Challenge these proposals under ONLY the supplied task rubric. Evaluate each criterion pass/fail/unknown with score 0–5 and a concrete rationale. Use unknown for evidence gaps, fail for established constraint violations. Entailment concerns reported facts, not proof of an explicitly untested prediction. An unmeasured bottleneck can be the first falsification question; do not demand a proven speedup before proposing a test. No-GPU-execution constraints prohibit launching jobs in this research session, not describing a later bounded experiment. A full-text source existing does not mean the quoted passage supports the claim. No search hits is uncertainty. Novelty is irrelevant unless explicitly enabled by the rubric. Score 0–5 with explicit objections. High polish must not rescue a failed gate. Known techniques, feature activation and ports are legitimate candidates if useful relative to the baseline. An explicit cheap baseline/eligibility check can resolve unknown deployment inclusion before implementation. Do not repeat historical novelty rejection as a current veto. Review each ID exactly once.

The first\nflash_mla_with_kvcache\ncall of each type runs FlashMLA's tile-scheduler planner, and the later layers of that type reuse the plan (the comment calls it \"shared across all ~60 layers of that type within a decode step\").\nHowever, the builder runs once per KV cache group. With the packed KV cache layout, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash's sliding-window caches are spread over 8 groups, and each group builds its own holders. So the planner runs once per (group, layer type). A torch-profiler trace of one TP8 decode step shows 18 planner launches in the target model (before layers 0-9 and 20-27) and 3 in the DSpark drafter (one per draft layer), where 4 are enough.\nget_mla_metadata_kernel\nis a single 32-thread block with a serial loop over the SM partitions, and every launch sits in front of its layer's attention. On H20 it takes 16.6 \u00b5s per launch at 1 request and about 41 \u00b5s at 64 requests.\nA plan depends on the batch shape and on the values of the decode\ntopk_length\n/\nextra_topk_length\n. FlashMLA's interface allows reuse only while those values stay the same, but it asserts only shapes and config. Across the KV cache groups of one metadata build pass the values do match:\nThe SWA lengths come from the same query and sequence lengths, window, valid-token mask and\nreplay_start\n. Each group builds the mask from its own slot mapping, but every sliding-window group writes each scheduled token, so the masks agree;\nreplay_start\nis one tensor passed to every builder.\nThe top-k lengths count the valid entries each index layer writes. Every decode top-k path fills the entries past a row end derived from\nseq_lens\nand\nnext_n\nalone with -1 (for example\nrowEnd\nin\ncsrc/libtorch_stable/sampler.cu\n), under the same valid-token mask. So each index layer of a compress ratio yields\nmin(rowEnd, topk)\n.\nThat second point is also why a group's same-type layers can already share one plan today, though they take their top-k from different index layers.\nThis PR lets the groups share the holders:\nMRV2's\nbuild_attn_metadata\npasses one dict to every group's\nCommonAttentionMetadata\n, next to the existing\n_token_to_req_indices_cache\n.\nThe sliding-window builder takes its plan holders from that dict, keyed by its block size, causality and index width.\nWhat stays as before:\nCallers that do not pass the dict (the V1 runner, the draft metadata refresh of the autoregressive speculator) keep one plan per group.\nDBO micro-batches and the DSpark drafter build in separate passes, so they never share a plan.\nSM100 does not take this path: it defaults to FlashMLA mega attention, which does not use these plan holders.\nPlanner launches per decode step drop from 21 to 4.\nDuplicate check.\ngh pr list --repo vllm-project/vllm --state all --search\nfor\ntile_sched\n,\nFlashMLASchedMeta\n,\nget_mla_metadata\n,\ntile scheduler\nand\nplanner flashmla\nshows no PR that shares the plan across KV cache groups.\nRelated:\n#58762\n(RFC\n#58638\n) reuses Mamba/GDN metadata across KV cache groups in the same\nbuild_attn_metadata\nloop. This adds an opt-in path that gathers the\ncandidate K from the paged FP8 indexer cache and scores only the candidates.\nImplements the alternative-A direction of\n#61830\n.\nNot a duplicate:\n#57858\nand\n#57859\nimplement candidate-only scoring for ROCm\n(gfx950); this is the SM90/CUDA counterpart called for in\n#61830\n.\nClaims\nClaiming only what is measured at this stage:\nAt ~1M context, 9 concurrent requests on 8x H20 (TP8): the four\ncandidate-consuming indexer layers drop from\n9.57 ms to 3.05 ms\nper step,\nand per-step time from\n39.04 ms to 31.53 ms\n(profiler-derived).\nSelected top-k sets are equivalent to the existing dense score-then-mask\npath.\nDefault behaviour is unchanged: the feature is off by default, and\nunsupported shapes or platforms fall back to dense rather than failing.\nKnown cost: a single-request step is about\n11.6% slower\nthan dense, since\nthe candidate path carries a per-step fixed cost. The discarded work grows linearly with context length\n(~98% of the scoring work at 1M context, per\n#61830\n).\nWhat the new path does.\nGather each candidate block's K from the paged FP8 indexer cache into a\ncompact per-row buffer.\nScore only the gathered candidates with the existing non-paged FP8 scoring\nkernel.\nRemap the selected columns back to request-local token positions, so the\noutput is identical in meaning to the dense path.\nScope kept narrow on purpose.\nOnly the candidate-consuming indexers take\nthe new path; the candidate-source and pre-candidate indexers stay dense, as\nthey have no candidate set to exploit. Restricted to SM90 with an FP8 indexer\ncache and no context parallelism \u2014 SM100 already has native sparse kernels,\nand all other platforms keep today's behaviour.\nTradeoff, and why the current gating is provisional.\nDense cost scales\nwith\nrows x context\n, while candidate scoring is dominated by a per-step\nfixed cost, so the crossover tracks scanned work relative to the candidate\nwidth rather than context length alone. "Conversation\nluoyuctl\ncommented\nSep 30, 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nContributor\nPurpose\nOn SM90, FlashMLA's sparse decode tiles query heads by 64 (the WGMMA M dimension). A DeepSeek V4.1 TP8 rank owns only 8 heads (16 at TP4), so it pads them to 64 and spends most of the tensor-core work on padding. This adds a Triton sparse decode kernel that tiles heads by 16 with\ntl.dot\n(mma.sync), reads the existing packed FP8 MLA cache directly, and recovers parallelism by splitting the key set across programs, merging the partial results with log-sum-exp.\nWhy it is faster at small batches.\nAt a few decode tokens per step, FlashMLA launches roughly one CTA per token and each CTA does 64-head work for 8 real heads. This kernel launches\ntokens \u00d7 splits\nprograms, each doing 16-head work over its share of keys. The best split count depends on both token count and key width: 32 splits at \u22643 tokens fills the GPU, but by 8 tokens 8 splits wins because the merge cost and per-program gather overhead dominate.\n_pick_config\nencodes this measured table. At larger batches the step becomes gather/dequant-bound rather than MMA-bound, so removing the padding no longer helps and FlashMLA is kept.\nDispatch\n(automatic, no flag): SM90, 8 or 16 local heads, decode only, and\nLayer key width (SWA + compressed top-k)\nUses this kernel at\nOtherwise\n\u2264256 (SWA-only layers)\n\u226416 decode tokens per step\nFlashMLA\n>256 (compressed layers,\nindex_topk=512\n)\n\u226412 decode tokens per step\nFlashMLA\nThe gate is decode tokens per rank per step (\u2248 batch \u00d7 (1 + spec tokens)), so the gain is limited to\nlow-concurrency decode\n. It does not depend on context length, since sparse attention reads a fixed number of keys per token. Prefill always uses FlashMLA.\nJIT warmup:\nboth launches register every split config,\nHAS_EXTRA\nvariant and DSpark SWA width with the startup JIT warmup, so no Triton compile happens on the serving path.\nRelated to\n#48168\n.\nNot a duplicate:\nI checked the issue discussion and open PRs for\n#48168\nand for small-head sparse decode; none implements this SM90 decode path.\n#57673\nhas since been reworked into an opt-in backend that reuses this PR's decoder (with attribution) but does not add the automatic FlashMLA dispatch.\n#55866\ntargets FA4 sparse MQA on\nBlackwell\n,\n#51403\ntargets\nROCm\n.\nTest Plan\npytest tests/kernels/attention/test_flashmla_sparse.py -k small_head -q\npython benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_dsv41_small_head_decode.py --heads 8 16 --topk 0 64 512 --rounds 3\npre-commit run --all-files\nEnd-to-end on 8\u00d7 H20 (one node), TP8, DSpark K=2, same flags as our production deployment except\n--max-model-len 32768\n. On SM90 those kernels do not exist, so consumers\n+currently score the whole context with the dense paged MQA-logits kernel and\n+then mask the logits down to the candidate blocks, discarding ~98% of the\n+scoring work at long context.\n+\n+This module provides the SM90 substitute for decode: gather each row's\n+candidate K from the paged FP8 indexer cache into a contiguous workspace,\n+score it with the existing dense (non-paged) MQA-logits kernel, run the\n+usual decode top-k, and remap the selected columns back to request-local\n+compressed positions. For unique valid candidates this preserves the scoring\n+problem without requantizing K. Different scoring kernels and top-k tie\n+breaking can still change floating-point results or the selected indices.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+import torch\n+\n+from vllm.logger import init_logger\n+from vllm.model_executor.kernels.attention.dsa.sparse_mqa_logits import (\n+    _sparse_topk_remap_kernel,\n+    candidate_blocks_to_sparse_indices,\n+    pick_sparse_block_kv,\n+)\n+from vllm.model_executor.layers.indexer_topk import (\n+    RADIX_TOPK_WORKSPACE_SIZE,\n+    get_indexer_topk,\n+)\n+from vllm.triton_utils import tl, triton\n+from vllm.utils.deep_gemm import fp8_fp4_mqa_logits\n+from vllm.v1.worker.workspace import current_workspace_manager\n+\n+logger = init_logger(__name__)\n+\n+\n+@triton.jit(\n+    do_not_specialize=[\n+        \"cache_block_stride\",\n+        \"cache_block_size\",\n+        \"num_cols\",\n+        \"bt_row_repeat\",\n+    ]\n+)\n+def _gather_candidate_k_kernel(\n+    kv_bytes,  # On SM100 that is DeepGEMM's sparse MQA-logits kernels.\n+SM90 has no equivalent, so the consumers score the whole compressed context\n+with the dense paged kernel and then mask the logits down to the candidate\n+blocks, discarding the work outside them. With a 16K-position candidate set\n+against a 1M-position context that is ~98% of the scoring.\n+\n+This module scores only the candidate positions, reading K **in place** from\n+the paged FP8 indexer cache. Logits land in a compact ``[rows, num_cands *\n+candidate_block_size]`` buffer whose columns are candidate-relative, the usual\n+decode top-k runs over that buffer, and the selected columns are remapped back\n+to request-local compressed positions.\n+\n+Two properties keep replays correct under full CUDA-graph capture:\n+\n+* **No per-step host decision.** Context lengths stay on the device; the host\n+  only sees shapes that are fixed by the model and scheduler config. A full\n+  CUDA graph therefore cannot bake a context-dependent choice, and replays\n+  stay correct at any live context length.\n+* **K is read in place.** Unlike a scheme that gathers candidate K into a\n+  scratch buffer first, no K data is copied that the dense path would not\n+  also read. The only extra work is per-row candidate bookkeeping (expansion\n+  and sorting of block ids), index arithmetic paid only while the path is\n+  active.\n+\n+Numerics follow the decode reference (``fp8_paged_mqa_logits``): dot, relu,\n+head weights, sum over heads, then one dequantization multiply per column.\n+Scaling after the relu is exact because the per-position scale is positive,\n+and it costs ``num_heads`` times fewer multiplies.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+import torch\n+\n+from vllm.logger import init_logger\n+from vllm.model_executor.kernels.attention.dsa.sparse_mqa_logits import (\n+    _sparse_topk_remap_kernel,\n+    candidate_blocks_to_sparse_indices,\n+)\n+from vllm.model_executor.layers.indexer_topk import (\n+    RADIX_TOPK_WORKSPACE_SIZE,\n+    get_indexer_topk,\n+)\n+from vllm.triton_utils import tl, triton\n+from vllm.v1.worker.workspace import current_workspace_manager\n+\n+logger = init_logger(__name__)\n+\n+# One fp32 scale per cached position, stored after the block's value bytes.\n+INDEXER_SCALE_BYTES = 4\n+# The same value for use inside the kernel; Triton only reads globals that are\n+# instantiated as constexpr.\n+_SCALE_BYTES = tl.constexpr(4)\n+\n+# Compact columns per program. The new\npath is explicitly restricted to SM90 and is not enabled automatically.\nNote\nAlternative host-offload path (not part of this pr):\nThe packed interface also enables a separate offload path in which gathering and packing are performed on the host, followed by transferring only the packed selected-KV workspace to the device. In this case, the full KV caches of the shared layers no longer need to remain resident in HBM. This can substantially expand KV-cache capacity.\nSince this implementation introduces a compile-time packed-KV specialization in the existing SM90 sparse decode kernel, I\u2019d appreciate maintainers\u2019 guidance on whether you prefer the current shared-kernel approach, a policy/loader-based abstraction, or a separate packed-kernel implementation; I\u2019m happy to restructure it in a follow-up revision based on your feedback.\nMotivation\nGLM-5.2 can share selected physical indices across multiple MLA layers. When\nthose indices are available before the layer that consumes them, the runtime\ncan move sparse address resolution and KV gathering into an earlier overlap\nwindow.\nThe attention kernel can then read a contiguous packed workspace instead of\nrepeatedly:\nloading and validating physical indices;\ntranslating indices into page and in-page coordinates;\nconstructing unrelated global addresses; and\ngathering NoPE, scale, and RoPE components from the paged cache.\nPacking and attention are intentionally separate operations so a runtime can\noverlap packing with preceding work such as MoE communication.\nWhat changed\nSM90 packing kernel\npack_selected_kv\ncopies each selected 656-byte V3.2 record byte-for-byte:\n512-byte FP8 E4M3 NoPE data;\n16-byte FP32 scales; and\n128-byte BF16 RoPE data.\nThe packer writes an opaque 64-token tile-major workspace. It supports a valid\nselected-index prefix described by\ntopk_length\nand zero-fills invalid suffix\nrecords.\nCallers may provide reusable contiguous\nuint8\nstorage using\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\n.\nPacked attention specialization\nThe packed H=64 and H=128 instantiations reuse FlashMLA's existing:\nWGMMA and dequantization pipeline;\nonline softmax;\nDSM clustering for H=128;\nsplit accumulation;\nepilogue; and\ncombine kernel.\nThe specialization replaces the sparse index/page/gather loader with direct\ntile-major loads and preserves partial-tail masking.\nScheduler policy\nH=128 uses the same scheduler as native sparse decode.\nH=64 uses the compact request-local scheduler selected by the L20X\nexperiments. For the measured full-prefix\ntopk=2048\n, 132-SM\nB*S_q=16\nregime, it falls back to the faster native scheduler.\nThis scheduler is used only by the opt-in packed helper and does not change\nnative sparse decode. The benchmark includes an identical-scheduler control to\nisolate the packed-load kernel gain.\nPython API\nThis PR adds:\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\nget_packed_mla_metadata\npack_selected_kv\nflash_mla_with_packed_kvcache\nSupported scope\nThe initial path is intentionally limited to:\nSM90;\nV3.2 FP8 sparse KV records;\nd_qk=576\n;\nd_v=512\n;\nH in {64, 128}\n;\nMQA KV; and\ntop-k capacity divisible by 64.\nThe API fails closed for unsupported architectures and shapes. "https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/pull/3502", "title": "[Klaud Cold] Extract shared git/GitHub helpers into infx.git and infx.github packages", "body": "## Summary\n\nAudit all git and GitHub usage under `infx/` and refactor into two new shared subpackages:\n\n- **`infx/git/`** -- subprocess-based git helpers (`show_file`, `rev_parse`, `diff_added_lines`, `ls_tree_batch_read`, `run_git`, `show_stage`) plus a `GitRepo` class wrapping GitPython for workflow automation\n- **`infx/github/`** -- backward-compatible gh-CLI wrapper (moved from `infx/github.py`) plus `infx/github/client.py` with PyGithub helpers (`resolve_token`, `require_token`, `make_client`, `is_transient_error`, `retry_delay`)\n\nAll callers refactored to use shared helpers. Behavior is identical.\n\n## Audit table\n\n| File | Old usage | New helper |\n|------|-----------|------------|\n| `infx/github.py` | Module with gh-CLI `subprocess.run` | Converted to `infx/github/__init__.py` package (backward compat) |\n| `infx/github/client.py` | N/A (new) | PyGithub helpers extracted from `merge_with_reuse.py` |\n| `infx/git/repo.py` | N/A (new) | Subprocess helpers + `GitRepo` (from `GitOps`) |\n| `infx/matrix/plan.py` | `subprocess.run([\"git\", \"diff\", ...])`, `subprocess.run([\"git\", \"ls-tree\", ...])`, `subprocess.run([\"git\", \"cat-file\", ...])` | `infx.git.diff_added_lines()`, `infx.git.ls_tree_batch_read()` |\n| `infx/workflows/validate_perf_changelog.py` | `subprocess.run([\"git\", \"show\", ...])` | `infx.git.show_file()` |\n| `infx/workflows/prepare_perf_changelog_merge.py` | `subprocess.run([\"git\", \"show\", \":stage:path\"])` | `infx.git.show_stage()` |\n| `infx/workflows/recover_failed_ingest.py` | `subprocess.run([\"git\", ...])` for show/rev-parse/worktree/add/diff/etc | `infx.git.run_git()`, `infx.git.show_file()`, `infx.git.rev_parse()` |\n| `infx/workflows/merge_with_reuse.py` | Inline `GitOps` class, `_resolve_token()`, `_make_github()`, `_is_transient_error()`, `_retry_delay()` | `infx.git.repo.GitRepo`, `infx.github.client.*` |\n| `infx/workflows/reuse.py` | `os.environ.get(\"GH_TOKEN\") or os.environ.get(\"GITHUB_TOKEN\")` | `infx.github.client.require_token()` |\n| `infx/workflows/reuse_comment.py` | Same inline token check | `infx.github.client.require_token()` |\n| `infx/klaud/__main__.py` | `subprocess.check_output([\"git\", \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"])` | `infx.git.rev_parse()` |\n| `infx/klaud/github.py` | `subprocess.check_output([\"gh\", \"api\", ...])` for binary zip download | Kept as subprocess (binary endpoint, not JSON) |\n\n## Dependency decision\n\n**GitPython and PyGithub remain in the `workflows` optional extra** (`pyproject.toml` unchanged). The `infx/git/` subprocess-based helpers (`show_file`, `rev_parse`, `diff_added_lines`, `ls_tree_batch_read`) work without GitPython and are safe for `--no-project` CI runs (used by `matrix/plan.py` and `validate_perf_changelog.py`). The `GitRepo` class and `infx/github/client.py` require their respective libraries and are importable only under the `workflows` extra. This avoids adding GitPython to the core install footprint while keeping a clean import boundary.\n\n## Token safety\n\n- Tokens are never passed on the command line, logged, printed, or embedded in URLs\n- `resolve_token()` reads from env vars or `gh auth token` captured in memory only\n- `require_token()` checks env vars only (no CLI fallback) for CI strictness\n- PyGithub/GitPython/urllib3 debug logging suppressed at WARNING level\n- Test added asserting tokens do not appear in error message text or repr\n\n## Package shadowing\n\nPython 3 absolute imports ensure `import git` resolves to GitPython and `import github` resolves to PyGithub even from within `infx/git/` and `infx/github/`. Tests verify this.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] New tests in `infx/tests/git_tests/test_repo.py` (9 tests covering subprocess helpers, GitRepo, shadowing)\n- [x] New tests in `infx/tests/github_tests/test_client.py` (12 tests covering token resolution, transient errors, retry delay, token safety, shadowing)\n- [x] All existing tests pass: 1852 passed, 6 skipped (pre-existing skips)\n- [x] Ruff check passes (all stable rules per `infx/ruff.toml`)\n- [x] Ruff format check passes\n- [x] 14 pre-existing `test_pool_launcher_*` failures on macOS (bash 3) are unrelated\n\n## AI model disclosure\n\n- Claude Opus 4.6 -- all code changes, audit, tests, and PR preparation\n\n<details><summary>\u4e2d\u6587</summary>\n\n## \u6458\u8981\n\n\u5ba1\u8ba1 `infx/` -4.636062754629013}, {"id": "github:SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX:3333", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/pull/3333", "title": "[Klaud Cold] Add MI325X TP4 DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash vLLM AgentX arm with Engram host offload / \u65b0\u589e Engram \u4e3b\u673a\u5378\u8f7d\u7684 MI325X TP4 DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash vLLM AgentX \u81c2", "body": "## Summary\nAdds a **TP4** arm with **Engram CPU offload** to `dsv41flash-fp4-mi325x-vllm-agentic-dspark`, beside the existing TP8 arm, and repins the image.\n\n- New search-space row `tp: 4` at concurrency 1-32, mirroring the TP8 grid so the two are directly comparable.\n- `dsv41flash_fp4_mi325x_mtp.sh` now sets `--engram-config` **explicitly per TP**: `cpu_offload:false` at TP8, `cpu_offload:true` at TP4.\n- Image repinned from `nightly-eed1f3d0\u2026` to `nightly-3df4ae153eb385e27b52f26c81f8edb9e20b9984`.\n- New `--no-swa-bounded-replay` flag.\n\n## Why the image had to move\nThe pinned `nightly-eed1f3d0\u2026` tag **404s on Docker Hub**; it survives only on node caches. The new pin is the same upstream commit the MI355X arm moved to in #3326, on the plain ROCm channel that the gfx942 arms already use rather than the `rocm100` channel, which no gfx942 recipe has exercised.\n\nThat commit is also what makes this change possible: [vllm-project/vllm#57491](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57491) widened two `is_cuda()` gates to `is_cuda_alike()`, so `engram_config` finally resolves on gfx942. Previously this recipe had to omit the flag entirely.\n\n## Why `--engram-config` is now explicit on both arms\nOn builds carrying #57491, `cpu_offload` **defaults on** through `VLLM_PLE_CPU_OFFLOAD`. Bumping the image without setting it would silently move the TP8 tables to host memory and change that curve. Setting it explicitly to `false` at TP8 keeps the existing arm behaviourally identical, and `true` at TP4 is what makes TP4 fit.\n\n## Why `--no-swa-bounded-replay`\n[vllm-project/vllm#56227](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56227) turned SWA bounded replay on by default **between the two pins**. It relies on a window clamp that landed in the FlashInfer and FlashMLA kernels; the ROCm sparse SWA path only gained the `replay_start` kwarg. On gfx950 that crashed every point with `HSA_STATUS_ERROR_MEMORY_FAULT` at the first prefix hit carrying a replay start. **gfx942 runs the same ROCm sparse path**, so this recipe pre-empts it rather than burning a sweep to rediscover it. Prefix caching stays on.\n\n## Memory\nWith the Engram tables offloaded, resident weights are ~81 GiB per rank at TP4, from the per-rank fit across the measured H100 TP8, H200 TP4 and B200 TP2 points (256 GiB sharded plus 17.2 GiB replicated). On a 256 GB card at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.9` that leaves roughly **100 GiB of KV per GPU** after the indexer's 32 GiB `[batched-tokens, 1M]` logits buffer. TP8 with the tables resident is unchanged at roughly 73 GiB per rank.\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] `full-sweep-enabled` sweep: TP4 concurrency 1-32 AgentX + evals green\n- [ ] TP8 re-runs green on the new image and matches the prior curve\n- [ ] Confirm the c1 log shows the Engram offload line at TP4 and no offload at TP8\n\n<details><summary>\u4e2d\u6587</summary>\n\n## \u6458\u8981\n\u5728 "https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/pull/3332", "title": "[Klaud Cold] Add MI300X TP4 DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash vLLM AgentX arm with Engram host offload / \u65b0\u589e Engram \u4e3b\u673a\u5378\u8f7d\u7684 MI300X TP4 DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash vLLM AgentX \u81c2", "body": "## Summary\nAdds a **TP4** arm with **Engram CPU offload** to `dsv41flash-fp4-mi300x-vllm-agentic-dspark`, beside the existing TP8 arm, and repins the image.\n\n- New search-space row `tp: 4` at concurrency 1-32, mirroring the TP8 grid so the two are directly comparable.\n- `dsv41flash_fp4_mi300x_mtp.sh` now sets `--engram-config` **explicitly per TP**: `cpu_offload:false` at TP8, `cpu_offload:true` at TP4.\n- Image repinned from `nightly-eed1f3d0\u2026` to `nightly-3df4ae153eb385e27b52f26c81f8edb9e20b9984`.\n- New `--no-swa-bounded-replay` flag.\n\n## Why the image had to move\nThe pinned `nightly-eed1f3d0\u2026` tag **404s on Docker Hub**; it survives only on node caches. The new pin is the same upstream commit the MI355X arm moved to in #3326, on the plain ROCm channel that the gfx942 arms already use rather than the `rocm100` channel, which no gfx942 recipe has exercised.\n\nThat commit is also what makes this change possible: [vllm-project/vllm#57491](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57491) widened two `is_cuda()` gates to `is_cuda_alike()`, so `engram_config` finally resolves on gfx942. Previously this recipe had to omit the flag entirely.\n\n## Why `--engram-config` is now explicit on both arms\nOn builds carrying #57491, `cpu_offload` **defaults on** through `VLLM_PLE_CPU_OFFLOAD`. Bumping the image without setting it would silently move the TP8 tables to host memory and change that curve. Setting it explicitly to `false` at TP8 keeps the existing arm behaviourally identical, and `true` at TP4 is what makes TP4 fit.\n\n## Why `--no-swa-bounded-replay`\n[vllm-project/vllm#56227](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56227) turned SWA bounded replay on by default **between the two pins**. It relies on a window clamp that landed in the FlashInfer and FlashMLA kernels; the ROCm sparse SWA path only gained the `replay_start` kwarg. On gfx950 that crashed every point with `HSA_STATUS_ERROR_MEMORY_FAULT` at the first prefix hit carrying a replay start. **gfx942 runs the same ROCm sparse path**, so this recipe pre-empts it rather than burning a sweep to rediscover it. Prefix caching stays on.\n\n## Memory\nWith the Engram tables offloaded, resident weights are ~81 GiB per rank at TP4, from the per-rank fit across the measured H100 TP8, H200 TP4 and B200 TP2 points (256 GiB sharded plus 17.2 GiB replicated). On a 192 GB card at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.9` that leaves roughly **60 GiB of KV per GPU** after the indexer's 16 GiB `[batched-tokens, 1M]` logits buffer. TP8 with the tables resident is unchanged at roughly 73 GiB per rank.\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] `full-sweep-enabled` sweep: TP4 concurrency 1-32 AgentX + evals green\n- [ ] TP8 re-runs green on the new image and matches the prior curve\n- [ ] Confirm the c1 log shows the Engram offload line at TP4 and no offload at TP8\n\n<details><summary>\u4e2d\u6587</summary>\n\n## \u6458\u8981\n\u5728 For a cached dispatch the handle already fixes that order: the same slots are refilled, the receive counts are the same, and `topk_idx` is asserted immutable. This stores the permutation on the handle next to `cached_recv_src_metadata_before_sort` at the first dispatch and reuses it, so cached compact dispatch no longer builds the sort keys, masks the unused capacity, or sorts.\n\nThe stored permutation follows the same stream and lifetime rules as the handle's existing cached metadata, so no per-call checks are needed and the reuse also holds under CUDA Graph capture and for deferred epilogues waited on another stream. Each handle keeps one extra int64 tensor of capacity rows (128 KiB at 16k rows). No API, default, native or test-file changes; `tests/ep/test_ep.py` already compares repeated cached dispatch against the reference, and the EP8 suite passes on this branch with CPU counts (direct and deferred epilogues) and in the sync-free mode.\n\nMeasured on 8\u00d7H200 (PyTorch 2.13.0+cu132), one cached dispatch per rank, 2048 tokens/rank, hidden 4096, top-2, BF16, deterministic, `do_cpu_sync=False`, under both `EP_AVOID_RECORD_STREAM` policies; outputs (payload, `topk_idx`, weights, source index) are bitwise identical to main across 608 fresh/cached/deferred/graph cases per policy:\n\n| Per cached dispatch | main | this PR |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| Device kernels and memsets | 24 | 6 |\n| Host enqueue time | 328 \u00b5s | 129 \u00b5s |\n| Synchronized wall time, 8-rank max | 439 \u00b5s | 280 \u00b5s |\n| Kernels in a captured cached dispatch | 15 (incl. sort) | 6 |\n\nThe removed kernels (radix sort, `arange`, compare, `clone`, `masked_fill`, memsets) account for about 46 \u00b5s of device time per call; the wall-time reduction is mostly host overhead and is a component measurement, not a training-throughput claim. Markdown\nSummary\nThis PR pipelines direct NVLink dispatch using three shared-memory TMA buffers per dispatch warp.\nPreviously, a dispatch warp prepared tokens sequentially around remote S2G stores. With this change, the next token's hidden data, scale factors, metadata, and routing information are prefetched while previous remote stores remain in flight.\nAt steady state, the three buffers are used for:\nA previous token whose remote S2G store may still be in flight.\nThe current token being sent to remote ranks.\nThe next token being prefetched from global memory.\nBefore recycling the oldest buffer,\ntma_store_wait<1>()\nreduces the number of outstanding store groups to one. This keeps buffer reuse safe without fully draining all remote stores between tokens.\nThe shared-memory calculation now accounts for all three staging buffers, and direct NVLink dispatch is limited to at most 14 dispatch warps. The non-NVLink path remains single-buffered.\nValidation\nTested on 8 x NVIDIA H100 GPUs with direct NVLink dispatch.\nCUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7 \\\nEP_DISABLE_GIN=1 \\\npython -u tests/elastic/test_ep.py \\\n  --num-processes 8 \\\n  --allow-hybrid-mode 0 \\\n  --test-first-only \\\n  --ignore-local-traffic\nAverage across 8 ranks:\nMetric\nmain\nThis PR\nChange\nDispatch\n250GB/s\n280 GB/s\n30\nCombine\n300 GB/s\n300 GB/s\n-\nWith rank unbalanced ratio set to 1.5:\nMetric\nmain\nThis PR\nChange\nDispatch\n203GB/s\n220 GB/s\n17\nCombine\n209 GB/s\n209 GB/s\n-\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\n\u7384\u6b66\nadded\n2\ncommits\nJuly 22, 2026 12:58\nperf: double-buffer direct NVLink dispatch\nb9ef9b3\nperf: prefetch direct dispatch tokens across NVLink stores\nd2be99a\nds-review-bot\nreviewed\nJul 22, 2026\nView reviewed changes\nComment thread\ndeep_ep/include/deep_ep/impls/dispatch.cuh\nOutdated\nComment on lines\n+264\nto\n+277\nconstexpr int kNumTmaBuffers = kIsScaleupNVLink ? 3 : 1), while the host sizes smem and caps warps using a separate kNumDirectNVLinkTmaBuffers/num_tma_buffers. The reuse-safety of round-robin prefetch (overwriting a staging buffer whose consuming remote store may still be in flight) relies on a non-local invariant: the count must exceed the number of distinct store groups that remain after tma_store_wait<1()> (here 1 remaining younger group, on a different buffer). This breaks if the two sources of truth diverge or the count changes. Template the count into the kernel and static-assert the invariant (or at least kNumTmaBuffers > 2).\n\ud83e\udd16 v4\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nComment thread\ndeep_ep/include/deep_ep/impls/dispatch.cuh\nOutdated\nComment on lines\n+378\nto\n+382\n// to the group that this wait just retired.\nptx::tma_store_wait<1>();\n__syncwarp();\nprepare_token(next_token_idx, next_token_iter, next_dst_rank_idx, next_dst_slot_idx);\n}\nds-review-bot\nJul 22, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nCollaborator\nThere was a problem hiding this comment.\nChoose a reason for hiding this comment\nThe reason will be displayed to describe this comment to others.\nLearn more\n.\nChoose a reason\nSpam\nAbuse\nOff Topic\nOutdated\nDuplicate\nResolved\nLow Quality\nHide\ncomment\n\ud83d\udd35\nsuggestion\n: The comment claims the next-token buffer is the group 'just retired' by the tma_store_wait<1>(). The internal dispatch copy epilogue still runs. This is not borrowed communication-buffer storage or a zero-copy dispatch implementation.\n\n## Regression coverage\n\nThe new `tests/ep/test_recv_lifetime.py` uses the independent NCCL dispatch reference and requires bitwise equality of payloads, local expert indices, routing weights, source-token metadata, and the saved-activation weight gradient.\n\nEach process run covers:\n\n- Deterministic dispatch off/on; output allocation on the compute/communication stream.\n- Uneven inputs, balanced routes, masked routes, and highly skewed routing with empty receivers.\n- Two selected experts on the same destination rank, distinguishing unique receive rows from token/expert assignments.\n- A prefix saved with `ctx.save_for_backward`, followed by three additional dispatches through the same buffer before backward.\n- A delayed reader on another stream, dropping the original receive allocation references and allocating/filling four same-capacity tensors on the allocation stream. The copy destination and allocator warm-up are prepared before the delay. Pressure writes must finish while a **pre-read** event is still pending; a pending post-copy event alone is insufficient.\n\nScheduling can miss the pressure window. All ranks agree on numerical correctness and window validity, then retry in collective lockstep with a longer delay (at most three attempts). Actual corruption fails immediately; an inconclusive window is never silently reported as coverage.\n\nThe consumer handoff uses a CUDA event captured **after the first dispatch-event wait**, including its deterministic-sort hook. It does not depend on repeated waits on the original `EventOverlap`; that is separate from this lifetime coverage.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Current polished revision\n\nHead: `97fa6d18ff717df99acbdbb862325bda3d4a0d46`, based on public main `b5a33eefda39485f438dc764695b71ba7b9249b6`.\n\nThe current 166-line test passed on H200 with PyTorch `2.13.0+cu132`, CUDA 13.2 and NCCL 2.32.3. Each native lifetime policy was run once with eight ranks: 24 distributed saved-prefix/backward cases in total, with 24 valid delayed-consumer allocator-pressure windows. link\nCopy Markdown\nCollaborator\nWith the evolution of hardware, networking, and model architectures, the previous DeepEP V1 had accumulated too much legacy baggage and performance issues. Today, we are excited to introduce\nDeepEP V2\n, which includes a complete refactoring of Expert Parallelism \u2014 achieving extreme performance with several times fewer SM resources compared to V1, while supporting significantly larger scale-up and scale-out domains \u2014 as well as experimental 0 SM Engram, 0 SM Pipeline Parallelism, and 0 SM Context Parallelism all-gather.\nWe are also happy to announce that we have switched from the NVSHMEM backend to the more lightweight\nNCCL Gin backend\n.\nNew Features\nFully JIT (Just-In-Time compilation)\nNCCL Gin backend\nHeader-only & lightweight\nAble to reuse existing NCCL communicators\nEPv2\nCUDA graph compatible, or faster CPU sync in saving memory mode\nHigh-throughput and low-latency APIs unified into a single interface, with a new GEMM layout\nLarger scale-up & scale-out domain support (up to EP2048)\nAnalytical SM & QP count calculation \u2014 no more auto-tuning needed\nBoth hybrid & direct modes remain supported\nFor V3-like legacy training, SM usage reduced from 24 to 4 - 6 while maintaining equivalent or better performance\n0 SM Engram (with RDMA)\n0 SM PP (with RDMA)\n0 SM CP (with Copy Engine)\nNotes\nBuffer size consumption is larger than V1\n0 SM RDMA low-latency EP is no longer supported\nEngram, PP, and CP are experimental features\nStill On-going Features\nElastic GPU & CPU buffers\n: A contiguous virtual address space that maps to a hybrid of GPU and CPU physical memory under the hood, enabling fully automatic and transparent Engram or imbalanced EP\nReducing intermediate buffer sizes by leveraging EP replay to handle load imbalance\nAll-gather updates and reduce-scatter implementations for DP & TP\nPerformance\nFollowing V3's configuration, we tested with 8K tokens per batch, 7168 hidden dimensions, top 8 experts, FP8 dispatching, and BF16 combining, and obtained the following results:\nArch\nNIC type\nTopo\nDispatch Bottleneck Bandwidth\nCombine Bottleneck Bandwidth\n#SMs\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n81 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 4\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n6\nSM100\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n91 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n726 GB/s (NVLink)\n740 GB/s (NVLink)\n64 (Max perf)\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n643 GB/s (NVLink)\n675 GB/s (NVLink)\n24 (Min #SM)\nNotes, the results are logical bandwidth. A may have a padded row stride. The A loader uses 16-byte `cp.async` copies with the existing shared-memory swizzle; B/output retain TMA. not exhaustive formal reviews or paginated replies", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/pull/448", "html_sha256": "fcb5d9b2e04e714f4c9908c0ec5ba16d58146821f9f0a989a22c6beafe03cc1f", "text_sha256": "e7390ce03590bd89e2b2b41c8ec0323433bd353ed78e27f95a440fc706d63caf", "characters": 10939, "title": "[BugFix][sm90] tf32 gemm race condition by leevan \u00b7 Pull Request #448 \u00b7 deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM \u00b7 GitHub", "patch_status": 200, "patch_sha256": "c56babb5ab83cd49e2d2f8d98fa07f612ade84fff0ab9734d4224a743ebd61c8", "patch_commit_shas": ["6bc1ce37cdae4b10b621eb0d69ac61185f1d60eb"], "changed_paths": ["deep_gemm/include/deep_gemm/impls/sm90_tf32_hc_prenorm_gemm.cuh", "tests/test_hyperconnection.py"]}, "content_hash": "f9292cd99cd6a8c711fc56ef1547c89cddbf49643a3c88db1cc08d63ac11ee88", "rank": -7.630114836417313}, {"id": "github:deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM:469", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/pull/469", "title": "Fix cross-expert output overwrites in SM90 masked GEMM", "body": "SM90 masked GEMM flattens the output into a 2D TMA descriptor. When a store tile extends past an expert's capacity, it can overwrite valid rows of the next expert. This affects both BF16 and FP8: capacity 32 reproduces it, and capacity 96 also fails when experts are nearly full.\n\nKeep the expert dimension in the output descriptor and reuse the existing 3D TMA store path. This preserves tile selection and the compute pipeline without adding buffers or synchronization. Boundary regressions are integrated into the existing BF16 and FP8 test suites.\n\nThis revisits the limitation discussed in #15 and the small-capacity failure reported in #268. #15 previously fixed small capacities by shrinking the store tile and rejected larger capacities not divisible by the tile size; it also suggested 3D TMA but deferred support for those cases. This change handles both small and non-tile-aligned capacities through the same output path.\n\nOn H200, the median paired latency changes across six rounds were between \u22120.17% and +0.16% for the larger tested shapes. The smallest shape was about 1.9% slower for BF16 and 1.5% slower for FP8, roughly 0.06\u20130.08 \u00b5s. The uniform 3D path avoids maintaining separate aligned and unaligned implementations, at that small measured cost.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Reproduction and validation</summary>\n\nRequires an SM90 GPU and a source build of DeepGEMM. Save the following as `repro.py` outside the source checkout and run `python repro.py` against the installed baseline or patched package:\n\n```python\nimport torch\nimport deep_gemm\nfrom deep_gemm.testing import calc_diff\n\ntorch.manual_seed(29)\na = torch.randn((8, 32, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\nb = torch.randn((8, 256, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\nd = torch.empty((8, 32, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\ncounts = torch.tensor([0, 1, 16, 31, 32, 1, 32, 32],\n                      device='cuda', dtype=torch.int)\nvalid = torch.arange(32, device='cuda')[None, :] < counts[:, None]\nreference = torch.bmm(a.double(), b.double().mT).bfloat16()\n\nfor _ in range(16):\n    deep_gemm.m_grouped_bf16_gemm_nt_masked(a, b, d, counts, 32)\n    diff = calc_diff(d[valid], reference[valid])\n    assert diff < 1e-5, f'{diff=}'\n```\n\nRunning this exact script against the baseline failed with `AssertionError: diff=tensor(0.1456, device='cuda:0', dtype=torch.float64)`; the patched wheel completed all 16 checks. Only valid rows are checked; padding values are unspecified.\n\nRun the included regressions from the checkout after building and installing the patched package:\n\n```bash\ncd tests\npython - <<'PY'\nimport torch\nimport test_bf16\nimport test_fp8_fp4\n\ntorch.manual_seed(17)\ntest_bf16.test_m_grouped_gemm_masked_boundaries()\ntest_fp8_fp4.test_m_grouped_gemm_masked_boundaries()\nPY\n```\n\nBoth passed with the final built wheel, including a fresh-JIT-cache run. Both fail at capacity 16 when run against the baseline package. A separate timed run with the JIT cache populated took 0.23 s for the BF16 test body and 0.09 s for FP8; this excludes process startup and imports, and does not estimate cold compilation time.\n\nSupplemental validation, using separate local harnesses not included in this PR:\n\n- Base: `057ca5964aae0879ff2e0eb71ee05a3cb0ba3df7`; candidate: `20865936a058d55c4c583cc2b0c2bcf3bc319a2f`. H200, PyTorch `2.13.0+cu130`, CUDA 13.0.\n- 34 configurations per arm, covering BF16 and FP8, capacities from 1 to 257, single-expert and all-empty cases; 16 eager and 16 Graph calls each. Baseline and revert each had 24 failing configurations; the fix had none. Two configurations were all-empty and only checked completion. The revert reused the original package in a fresh process/cache.\n- Independent FP64 matmul references, rounded to BF16; FP8 references used the actual quantized inputs and scales. No run-to-run changes were observed in the fixed panel.\n- 37 adjacent-path checks passed, covering ordinary GEMM layouts, output dtypes and accumulation, batched GEMM, and K-grouped GEMM.\n- Native `python setup.py bdist_wheel` passed with setuptools `75.8.0`.\n\nPerformance used six paired rounds with alternating arm order. Each measurement took the median of seven CUDA-event samples, each containing 1,000 Graph calls. Input generation and quantization were outside timing; FP8 API scale conversion was included. Positive values below mean slower; values are medians of paired latency changes.\n\n| Groups | Capacity | N | K | Expected M | BF16 | FP8 |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8 | 128 | 256 | 256 | 64 | +1.931% | +1.526% |\n| 32 | 4096 | 1536 | 2048 | 20 | +0.070% | \u22120.143% |\n| 6 | 4096 | 4096 | 4096 | 1024 | +0.155% | +0.023% |\n| 32 | 4096 | 6144 | 7168 | 192 | \u22120.167% | \u22120.056% |\n\nClocks were not locked; sampled SM clocks ranged from 1305 to 1980 MHz. Small differences on the larger shapes should not be interpreted as established speedups. Previously, the next iteration loaded its A fragment\nbefore waiting for the preceding iteration's WGMMA to complete. This could overwrite A registers while they were still being read by\nthe in-flight WGMMA.\nFix\nProposed fix:\n#448\nThe fix moves\nwarpgroup_wait<0>()\nimmediately after\nwarpgroup_commit_batch()\n, ensuring that each iteration's WGMMA completes\nbefore its A registers are reused.\nThe PR also adds a regression test covering split-K, PDL enabled/disabled, and default/side streams. It checks outputs against FP32\nreferences and verifies repeatability across identical launches.\nValidation\nWith the fix applied, both the existing hyperconnection tests and all 24 configurations of the new regression test passed on an SM90\nGPU.\nTODO\nfix will introduce some performance regression which suppose to be mitigated in upcoming PRs\u3002\nReactions are currently unavailable\nActivity\nLiRunGuo\ncommented\non\nSep 22, 2026\nLiRunGuo\non Sep 22, 2026\nMore actions\nI measured this on an H200 (SM90, driver 580.159.03, PyTorch 2.11.0+cu130) with DeepGEMM\nmain\n(\n78b6900\n), in case it helps the review of\n#448\n.\nReproduction.\nI ran the 24 shapes from\ntest_hc_prenorm_gemm\n(\nm\nin 13 / 137 / 4096 / 8192,\nn = 24\n,\nk\nin 7168 / 7680 / 28672, with and without 16 splits), with 50 back-to-back launches per shape. It looks for writes to the A registers of an in-flight\nHGMMA\ngroup before the\nWARPGROUP.DEPBAR\nthat waits for it, and found none: ptxas puts the A fragments of consecutive stages in different registers. So the source-level race is real, but these two ptxas versions do not turn it into a hazard for these instantiations. That may explain why it is hard to reproduce, and it could still show up with other compiler versions.\nPerformance of\n#448\n.\nMedian of 3\nbench_kineto\nruns, NVCC 12.9, in \u00b5s:\nm, k, splits\nmain\n#448\ndouble-buffered A\n137, 28672, none\n106.5\n120.8 (+13%)\n111.4 (+5%)\n4096, 7680, none\n32.9\n36.2 (+10%)\n33.8 (+3%)\n4096, 7680, 16\n45.5\n38.0 (\u221216%)\n47.6 (+5%)\n8192, 28672, none\n123.5\n131.8 (+7%)\n125.2 (+1%)\nThe geometric mean over all 24 shapes versus\nmain\nis +2.8% for\n#448\nand +2.2% for the double-buffered variant (NVCC 13.0: +4.6% and +2.9%).\n#448\ncosts 8\u201313% on most shapes without splits, but it is about 16% faster on the\nk = 7680\n, 16-split shapes.\nThe double-buffered variant keeps two A register buffers selected by stage parity, and waits with one WGMMA group in flight (\nwarpgroup_wait<1>\n) before a buffer is reused. It fixes the race by construction and recovers most of the loss on the shapes without splits, but it does not get\n#448\n's gain on the 16-split shapes. So it is not clearly better overall. SM89 is the first architecture below Hopper that has FP8 Tensor Cores.\n\nPreviously, SM80 and SM89 used the same path. The path required SM90 or higher for FP8. This was correct for SM80 because Ampere has no FP8 Tensor Cores. It was wrong for SM89. SM89 implements the instruction `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k32.row.col.f32.e4m3.e4m3.f32` (`QMMA.16832.F32.E4M3.E4M3`). The change separates the two architectures and gives SM89 a real kernel.\n\n## Why\n\n- SM89 has FP8 Tensor Cores. It does not have the SM90 features (no TMA, no `mbarrier.try_wait.parity`, no WGMMA, no clusters). It could not reuse the SM90 or SM100 kernels. It fell back to BF16 through cuBLASLt.\n- FP8 users on Ada had to dequantize and run BF16. They lost about 2\u00d7 of Tensor Core throughput.\n- The shared SM80/SM89 path also gave wrong error messages. An SM89 user saw a message that did not apply to their GPU.\n\n## Design\n\nSM89 has no TMA and no asynchronous barriers. The kernel uses the pre-Hopper primitives.\n\n- It uses `mma.sync` (wrapped in `deep_gemm/mma/sm89.cuh`, with A/B/C fragment layouts documented) and `ldmatrix.x4` for operand staging.\n- It uses a multi-stage `cp.async` pipeline with a 128-byte XOR swizzle.\n- It uses a persistent block scheduler (one CTA per SM). Tiles walk in a loop.\n- It uses direct global stores because SM89 has no TMA store. It has an alignment-safe scalar tail. An MMA lane owns a pair of adjacent columns. For odd N the element offset of a row can be odd. A 2-element vector store is then illegal. This caused the original misaligned address failure.\n- Block K is fixed at 128 because of the per-128-channel scaling granularity. Pipeline depth and tile shapes come from a new cost model in `csrc/jit_kernels/heuristics/sm89.hpp`. The warp split uses one shared function `get_mma_sync_warps_n()`. Host heuristics and device code both call it so they cannot disagree.\n\nBoth SM90 SF recipes are supported:  \n`(1, 1, 128)` (per-token A and B scales) and  \n`(1, 128, 128)` (per-token A, one B scale per 128-column N block).  \nThe second recipe is the default when FP32 scales are used. SM89 uses the same FP32 SF layouts as SM90. The function `transform_sf_into_required_layout` was extended. It was not duplicated.\n\n## Scope\n\nSupported features:  \ndense FP8 GEMM, K-major (NT) operands only, FP32 and BF16 outputs, `D = C + A @ B`, ragged M/N, `k % 16 == 0`, both aliases (`fp8_fp4_gemm_nt`, `fp8_gemm_nt`, `fp4_gemm_nt`), and shape specialization.\n\nStill rejected (with an explicit message that names the requirement):  \ngrouped and K-grouped FP8, MN-major FP8 operands, FP4, custom epilogues, `alpha` scaling, PDL, and MQA/attention.  \nBF16 keeps its cuBLASLt fallback.\n\n## Incidental fixes\n\n- `set_pdl()` now panics below SM90. It no longer sets a flag that `griddepcontrol` cannot honour.  \n  `cudaGridDependencySynchronize()` is wrapped in a no-op below `__CUDA_ARCH__ >= 900` so the SF-layout kernels compile for SM89.\n- `call_cublaslt_api` used `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F_FAST_TF32` for non-FP32 operands. That mode only down-casts FP32 inputs to TF32. BF16/FP8 operands ran at TF32 precision by mistake. Now only FP32 inputs use that mode.\n- A new `DG_PANIC` macro throws always and carries a message.\n\n## Bug found during validation\n\nThe persistent loop primed its `cp.async` pipeline only in the prologue before the tile loop. It relied on a cross-tile tail load to prime the next tile. A tile\u2019s tail can only reach the stages that are already free of the current tile \u2014 prologue stages `p \u2208 [0, kNumStages-2-num_k_blocks]`. The next tile\u2019s K loop consumes `p = 0..num_k_blocks-1`. When `num_k_blocks < kNumStages-1` (example: `k=512`, 4 blocks, 8 stages) the high stages were never reloaded. The next tile read the previous tile\u2019s shared memory. Symptom: non-deterministic `nan` and values near 1e30. The problem appeared only for small M spread over many N tiles.\n\nThe fix extracts the prologue into `prime_pipeline()` and re-primes unconditionally after the epilogue. The re-prime is guarded by `cp_async_wait<0>()` and a `__syncthreads()`. `cp.async` completion is per-thread, so both are required before the stages can be reused. The change removes a prologue/epilogue overlap that was never correct. Measured cost is about 0\u20133.5 % (4096\u00b3: 2518 \u00b5s vs 2507 \u00b5s, within noise; 4096\u00d74096\u00d7512: 420 \u00b5s vs 405 \u00b5s).\n\n`tests/test_sm89.py` added the shapes `(1, 4096, 512)`, `(64, 4096, 512)` and `(128, 4096, 128)`. Reverting the fix makes these tests fail (34 failures). They are real regression tests.\n\n## Testing\n\nHardware: RTX 4060 Laptop (SM89, 24 SMs, 7.6 GiB), CUDA 13.0, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130.\n\n- `tests/test_sm89.py` \u2014 17 tests, OK from a cold JIT cache (~130 s).  \n  Covers both recipes \u00d7 both output dtypes \u00d7 accumulate \u00d7 12 shapes, explicit `recipe=(1,128,128)`, `recipe_a`/`recipe_b` pairs, entry-point aliases, c", "created_at": "2026-10-14T19:01:11Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-16T18:30:02Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:23Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["h100", "throughput", "performance", "nvfp4"], "metadata": {"number": 474, "labels": [], "comments": 0, "state_reason": null}, "content_hash": "e9e726ee03fe75aa494d2982073d7cff644a759f6b2dc7cbce2a25919d9f2557", "rank": -8.646066354977142}, {"id": "github:deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM:411:live-page", "source": "github", "kind": "resource", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/pull/411", "title": "feat: add SM90 FP8 x MXFP4 MegaMoE support by huangzhilin-hzl \u00b7 Pull Request #411 \u00b7 deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM \u00b7 GitHub", "body": "Conversation\nhuangzhilin-hzl\ncommented\nAug 20, 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nco-author\uff1a\n@guzekai01\nInspired by the SM90 FP8\u00d7FP8 MegaMoE implementation introduced in\ndeepseek-ai/DeepGEMM#383\nand the SM90 FP8\u00d7NVFP4 work in\nAichenF/DeepGEMM@megamoe_nvfp4_dev_m\n, this PR adds an optimized FP8\u00d7MXFP4 MegaMoE backend for SM90 GPUs. Keeping routed-expert weights in MXFP4 enables native reuse of Hugging Face MXFP4 MoE checkpoints, while substantially reducing GPU memory pressure by avoiding a persistent FP8 weight expansion.\nSummary\nUnified SM90 persistent execution.\nUnlike\n[SM90] Add FP8 MegaMoE support\n#383\n, this implementation fuses dispatch, routed/shared L1 and L2, and combine into one persistent kernel. It adapts the SM100 ring-buffer and wave-interleaved scheduler to a cluster-free Hopper design using register-resident WGMMA, one CTA per task, and two resident CTAs per SM.\nHumming-inspired MXFP4\u00d7FP8 compute path.\nRouted weights remain packed MXFP4 in global memory. Model-load preprocessing rebases the K32 UE8M0 exponents into bounded relative scales plus one FP32 secondary scale per expert, following the fused MXFP4\u00d7FP8 approach used by Humming. During execution, packed E2M1 tiles are loaded into compact shared-memory staging, decoded and expanded on the fly into E4M3 FP8 tiles, and then consumed by Hopper WGMMA. This avoids materializing a persistent FP8 copy of the routed weights.\nBenchmark Results\nThe benchmark follows the H20 workload shapes and timing methodology from\n#383\n. referenced\n      this pull request\nSep 10, 2026\nfeat(sm120): DeepSeek-V4.1 (DSV4_1) sparse MLA cache format support\n\u2026\n2c71fb6\nDeepSeek-V4.1-Flash stores each KV token as 528B: 512B of FP8 E4M3 covering\nthe full 512-wide K (rope lanes quantized, no BF16 rope segment) plus a 16B\nfooter of 16 UE8M0 scales over 32-wide groups (the\ndeepseek-ai/FlashMLA#221\nlayout, SM100-only there). Add ModelType::DSV4_1 on top of the ScaleSpec\ngroundwork: GLM53_NOPE-style geometry (D_ROPE=0) composed with a 32-wide\nUE8M0 footer.\n\nKernel side the format is simpler than DSV4 (one 512B bulk per row, a single\nunified block-scaled FP8 QK pass, no rope MMA paths). The two new mechanics:\n\n- the 16B footer row generalizes the scale gather from one uint64_t to a\n  width-selected load (uint4) in both the prefill IO helper and the decode\n  kernel's staged gather;\n- the 32-wide groups constrain the XV W-fold (one scale group per W buffer),\n  so an 8-warp XV split would floor NT_PER_WARP_XV to 0: decode runs a\n  4-math-warp tile at BI=64 (the DOTS3_SWA halving precedent) and prefill is\n  SG-only on the BI=32 producer/consumer tile. The ComputeTraits/SmemLayout\n  aliases now take the XV warp count so the NT_PER_WARP_XV assert evaluates at\n  the warp count the XV MMA actually runs at.\n\nSelection is explicit everywhere (d_qk=512 collides with DSV4, the 528B\npayload with GLM53_NOPE): the functional API gains kv_scale_format=\n\"ue8m0_g32\", the TRTLLM-compat entry kv_cache_format=\"fp8_dsv41\", and the\ndecode-dsv4 FFI takes an explicit model_type (-1 keeps the legacy width\ninference, matching the paged-entry kAuto convention). Dual-cache decode is\nsupported like DSV4; dual-cache prefill (MG_DUAL) is intentionally not\ninstantiated. Calibration keys a new dsv4_1 family at topk 512.\n\nTests: decode matrix (dedicated/runtime-H heads, partial-tail topk, sinks),\nSG prefill, dual-cache through the TRTLLM entry, poisoned-slot-0 NaN safety\non both gather paths, dispatch/plan/envelope coverage. 666 passed on RTX PRO\n6000 (SM120).\n\nSigned-off-by: lucifer1004 "7e18c9147734080b1cefd2e856e3802575ea31fb5991e1f3c49e950f79590d8f", "rank": -6.108056263634463}, {"id": "github:deepseek-ai/FlashMLA:213:live-page", "source": "github", "kind": "resource", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/FlashMLA/pull/213", "title": "Reduce shared memory usage in decode scheduling metadata kernel by FrankMinions \u00b7 Pull Request #213 \u00b7 deepseek-ai/FlashMLA \u00b7 GitHub", "body": "Conversation\nFrankMinions\ncommented\nAug 24, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nSummary\nReduce dynamic shared memory in\nget_mla_metadata_kernel\nfrom\n5*b+1\nto\n3*b+1\nby dropping redundant\nfirst_block_idx\n/\nlast_block_idx\nbuffers.\nSince\nfirst_token_idx\nis always 0,\nfirst_block_idx\nis always 0 and\nlast_block_idx + 1\nequals\nnum_blocks\n, so block range bounds can be derived from\nnum_blocks\nand the local split offset (\nnow_block\n) instead of being cached in shared memory.\nUpdate\nbegin_block_idx\n/\nend_block_idx\n/\nis_last_req_splitted\ncomputation accordingly; scheduling semantics are unchanged.\nMotivation\nThe decode scheduling metadata kernel previously reserved shared memory for five\nint\narrays of size roughly proportional to batch size. Two of those arrays were redundant given the current dense/sparse indexing assumptions (\nfirst_token_idx == 0\n). "body": "Conversation\nLivinfly\ncommented\nAug 30, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nSummary\nThis PR adds optional per-request\ntopk_length\nsupport to the SM90\nDeepSeek-V3.2 FP8 sparse decode kernel (\nd_qk=576\n). SM90 V3.2 can now use\ndynamic top-k instead of always processing the full allocated top-k capacity.\nWhen\ntopk_length\nis provided, the kernel:\nloads the valid length for each batch row;\nschedules only\nmax(ceil_div(topk_length, TOPK_BLOCK_SIZE), 1)\nblocks;\nmasks a partial tail before its indices can be used for KV addressing; and\nincludes the absolute top-k position in the softmax validity predicate.\nWhen\ntopk_length == nullptr\n, the existing fixed top-k behavior is preserved.\nThere is no public API change, and MODEL1 and SM100 behavior are unchanged.\nWhy\ntemplate<bool DYNAMIC_TOPK, ...>\nI also tested a single-device-kernel implementation that checks\nparams.topk_length != nullptr\nat runtime. The condition is grid-uniform, but\nthe compiler cannot remove the length load, tail predicates, extra control\nflow, or their value lifetimes from the fixed path.\ntemplate<bool DYNAMIC_TOPK>\nmoves the choice to host launch dispatch:\nfalse\n: the dynamic-length logic is compiled out, preserving the fixed path;\ntrue\n: per-row block counts and partial-tail safety checks are enabled.\nThe comparison implementation is available at\n7b7c831\non\ntest/sm90-v32-dynamic-topk-runtime-device\n. It regresses the representative\nB64-B128 fixed cases by 8.4%-9.4%, while the specialized implementation stays\nwithin 0.5% of the baseline. Both builds report 168 registers, 12 barriers,\nand no spills, so the generated hot-path instructions\u2014not an occupancy\nchange\u2014explain the observed difference.\nCorrectness\npython tests/test_flash_mla_sparse_decoding.py\nFocused reference checks passed for:\nCase\nResult\nV3.2 dynamic H64 / H128\npass\nExisting MODEL1 dynamic H64 / H128\npass\nV3.2 tail lengths\n0,1,17,63,64,65,127,128\npass\nFor the tail-safety check, every suffix entry after\ntopk_length\nwas replaced\nwith\nINT_MAX\n. H64 and H128 matched the corresponding safe-tail results, with\nno illegal memory access.\nKernel performance\nEnvironment: NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3 (SM90), driver 580.126.09, nvcc 12.8.93,\nand PyTorch 2.13.0+cu129. Each number is the median of seven group means, with\n10 warmups and 20 timed calls per group using CUDA events. Scheduler metadata\nis reused and excluded from timing.\nThese are standalone FlashMLA sparse-decode measurements; they exclude input\ngeneration, index compaction, framework integration, collectives, and model\nexecution. B3/Q257 and mixed B4 still select backend and retain performance opportunities; bounded declarations do not guarantee the ideal candidate for every workload. These are layer measurements, not serving E2E.\n\nHost costs were measured separately with the same LSE-enabled calls. Additional capacity classes still need prewarming before capture and can require more cold compilation and split workspace.\n\nThese stock-FE measurements supersede the earlier provisional two-guard heuristic experiment in the PR description. -4.580111469043251}, {"id": "github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:6616", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/6616", "title": "feat(mla): read DeepSeek-V4.1 inline-scale KV records natively on SM120", "body": "## \ud83d\udccc Description\n\nFlashMLA's DeepSeek-V4.1 ABI (as of its 2026.09.30 release) stores each token as one self-contained record: each token's data is followed by its own scales. vLLM adopted it in vllm-project/vllm#61371. The SM120 sparse-MLA DSv4.1 path reads only footer-scale pages, in which a page holds all its tokens' data and then all their scales.\n\nThe two layouts have the same record size (528 B), so a cache in the other layout passes every shape check and decodes into silently wrong output. This PR adds the inline-scale records as new, explicit formats. The existing names keep their footer meaning.\n\n| | main (SWA) cache | FP4 compressed cache |\n|---|---|---|\n| Footer (`fp8_dsv41`, `ue8m0_g32`) | per page: P \u00d7 512 B E4M3, then P \u00d7 16 B UE8M0 | per page: P \u00d7 256 B E2M1, then P \u00d7 32 B E4M3 |\n| Inline (new) | per token: 512 B E4M3 + 16 B UE8M0 (group 32) = 528 B | per token: 256 B E2M1 + 32 B E4M3 (group 16) = 288 B |\n\nAs in FlashMLA, sparse indices address inline records as rows of the whole cache, and a page stride must be a whole number of rows.\n\n### API\n\n- **SM120 wrapper / functional API:** `kv_scale_format=\"ue8m0_g32_inline\"` selects the inline records; `\"ue8m0_g32\"` keeps the footer meaning.\n- **`trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla`:** adds `kv_cache_format=\"fp8_dsv41_inline\"` and `\"fp8_dsv41_inline_fp4_ca\"` (`backend=\"sparse\"`). `backend=\"cake\"` rejects them with a `ValueError`.\n- **Writers:** the DSv4.1 FP8/FP4 pack and append writers take `inline_scale=True`. They then write records at the cache's row pitch, and append slots address rows.\n\nThe names follow the existing conventions: inline-scale vs footer-scale vocabulary, and the record layout is part of the format identity rather than a separate argument.\n\n### Implementation\n\n- **New model type:** a `DSV4_1_INLINE` model on the DSV4_1 routes. Its record is the smem row, which DSV4_1 already pads to 528 B.\n- **One copy per candidate:** the gathers issue one bulk copy per candidate and drop the synchronous footer-scale load. The QK, value-dequant and W-fold stages read the scales at +512 in smem.\n- **Mixed-cache schedules:** they copy each 288 B FP4 record whole and convert it straight into the inline KV row.\n- **Full-BF16 kernels:** these load per thread, and address inline records through an inline layout that has the footer layouts' offset interface.\n- **Row strides:** rows of both caches are addressed flat with validated runtime row strides (16 B multiples of at least the record size), as for GLM53_NOPE. The attention metadata gains the extra cache's row stride, appended at the end so positional initializers keep working.\n- **Prep refactor:** `qk_k_scale_selector` and `kv_scale_fp32` are now keyed on `SCALE_FORMAT` instead of `SCALE_IN_KV_SMEM`, so an inline model may store UE8M0 bytes. Every existing model keeps its reads.\n- **Calibration:** gains a `dsv4_1_inline` family, keyed on the cache's own page size. No device profiles are committed.\n- **Bit-exact:** the math order is unchanged, so inline outputs and LSE equal the footer form's bit for bit.\n\n### Performance\n\nMeasured on an RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition with CUPTI kernel time, cold L2, median \u00b5s, on the same rows and indices. Each layout uses its own calibrated profiles. \"512\" is a single cache with top-k 512; \"128 + fp4\" is main top-k 128 plus 512 FP4 compressed candidates. Footer pages hold 64 tokens.\n\nDefault precision:\n\n| cache | heads | tokens | footer | inline | inline / footer |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 512 | 8 | 1 | 13.3 | 11.7 | 0.88 |\n| 512 | 64 | 1 | 14.1 | 12.7 | 0.90 |\n| 512 | 64 | 16 | 34.3 | 30.4 | 0.89 |\n| 512 | 64 | 64 | 85.4 | 78.3 | 0.92 |\n| 512 (prefill) | 64 | 1024 | 1058.9 | 961.5 | 0.91 |\n| 128 + fp4 | 8 | 1 | 14.8 | 12.4 | 0.84 |\n| 128 + fp4 | 64 | 16 | 40.4 | 34.3 | 0.85 |\n| 128 + fp4 | 16 | 64 | 49.0 | 39.4 | 0.80 |\n| 128 + fp4 | 64 | 64 | 111.6 | 101.8 | 0.91 |\n| 128 + fp4 (prefill) | 64 | 1024 | 1381.5 | 1160.2 | 0.84 |\n\nFull-BF16 compute precision:\n\n| cache | heads | tokens | footer | inline | inline / footer |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 512 | 64 | 64 | 117.1 | 112.6 | 0.96 |\n| 512 (prefill) | 64 | 1024 | 1259.2 | 1212.6 | 0.96 |\n| 128 + fp4 | 32 | 64 | 88.5 | 83.1 | 0.94 |\n| 128 + fp4 (prefill) | 64 | 1024 | 1644.2 | 1520.6 | 0.92 |\n\n- **Default precision:** inline is 4\u201320% faster than footer pages across all measured shapes.\n- **Full BF16:** inline is up to 8% faster. Small FP4 decode is the exception, within about 1% (one shape 1.2% slower). The BF16 kernels are compute-bound with per-thread loads.\n- **Row-stride cost:** making the extra cache's row stride a runtime value costs nothing measurable (after/before 0.997\u20131.001; BF16 1.000).\n- **Reproducing:** `benchmarks/bench_sparse_mla_sm120.py` has a DSv4.1 footer-vs-inline section.\n\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\n\n- vllm-project/vllm#61371: vLLM's interleaved V4.1 KV records (FlashMLA 2026.09.30).\n- vllm-project/vllm#59846: SM120 DSv4.1 decode through FlashInfer's V4.1 sparse MLA; indexer KV cache and packed group overlays", "body": "## Context\r\n\r\nWhen DeepSeek V4 uses MXFP4 indexer K values, engine reserves the larger FP8 row for them. Its packed KV planner also buckets layers by page size, leaving avoidable holes when different cache groups have different mixtures of page sizes.\r\n\r\nThis PR:\r\n\r\n- sizes MXFP4 indexer K rows from their packed values and UE8M0 scales (`head_dim / 2 + head_dim / 32` bytes), reducing the 128-dimensional row from 132 to 68 bytes;\r\n- lays out every packed cache group densely in one per-block slab and overlays the group layouts, while keeping layers within each group disjoint; and\r\n- removes cross-group SWA page padding that the dense planner no longer needs.\r\n\r\nThe packing change is generic to the existing packed-cache path. DeepSeek V4 uses that path by default; other hybrid models retain the existing opt-in via `enable_cross_layers_blocks`.\r\n\r\n## Memory results\r\n\r\nMatched DeepSeek-V4-Pro DEP8 startup measurements on GB200:\r\n\r\n| Backend / KV budget | Baseline blocks | This change | Increase |\r\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| FlashInfer sparse / 26.46 GiB | 21,709 | 23,120 | +6.50% |\r\n| FlashMLA sparse / 26.09 GiB | 19,015 | 21,306 | +12.05% |\r\n\r\nFor the FlashInfer layout, physical block stride decreases from 1,308,672 to 1,228,800 bytes (-6.10%). For the FlashMLA `fp8_ds_mla` layout, it decreases from 1,473,408 to 1,315,008 bytes (-10.75%).\r\n", "created_at": null, "updated_at": "2026-07-22T23:58:45Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T23:05:54Z", "state": "merged", "merged_at": "2026-07-22T23:58:45Z", "parent": null, "tags": ["deepseek", "kv-cache"], "metadata": {"number": 48993, "labels": [{"id": 7197733874, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAABrQSz8g", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/ready", "name": "ready", "color": "ededed", "default": false, "description": "ONLY add when PR is ready to merge/full CI is needed", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}, {"id": 8068298936, "node_id": "LA_kwDOI7xefs8AAAAB4Oh4uA", "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/vllm-project/vllm/labels/v1", "name": "v1", "color": "9810B3", "default": false, "description": "", "archived_at": null, "archived_by": null}], "comments": 1, "state_reason": null, "base": {"ref": "main", "sha": "431934522b65f126c43f74b19cdd8f3bf63f9747", "label": "vllm-project:main"}, "head": {"ref": "codex/dsv4-compact-packed-kv-public", "sha": "7f5cdd5fa87708087430537af90ca227f2af30dc", "label": "GirasoleY:codex/dsv4-compact-packed-kv-public"}, "merge_commit_sha": "f3a920a0764012555fa0d3b6559ac0ddeb746200", "draft": false, "changed_files": null, "additions": null, "deletions": null, "pr_detail_fetched_at": "2026-10-19T23:05:54Z", "import_origin": "/home/ubuntu/AutoResearch-Inference/ideation/runs/pr-evaluation-audit-001/inputs/corpus-opening-records.jsonl"}, "content_hash": "f9fd6c7078989ff7eb9f8076ec60106641bef1b9d4032f04bbf1b65756a41185", "rank": -14.26474159951275}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:58818", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58818", "title": "[Perf][DSv4.1] Share the FlashMLA decode plan across KV cache groups", "body": "## Purpose\r\n\r\n`DeepseekSparseSWAMetadataBuilder.build()` creates one empty `FlashMLASchedMeta` per DeepSeek-V4 layer type. The first `flash_mla_with_kvcache` call of each type runs FlashMLA's tile-scheduler planner, and the later layers of that type reuse the plan (the comment calls it \"shared across all ~60 layers of that type within a decode step\").\r\n\r\nHowever, the builder runs once per KV cache group. With the packed KV cache layout, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash's sliding-window caches are spread over 8 groups, and each group builds its own holders. So the planner runs once per (group, layer type). A torch-profiler trace of one TP8 decode step shows 18 planner launches in the target model (before layers 0-9 and 20-27) and 3 in the DSpark drafter (one per draft layer), where 4 are enough.\r\n\r\n`get_mla_metadata_kernel` is a single 32-thread block with a serial loop over the SM partitions, and every launch sits in front of its layer's attention. On H20 it takes 16.6 \u00b5s per launch at 1 request and about 41 \u00b5s at 64 requests.\r\n\r\nA plan depends on the batch shape and on the values of the decode `topk_length` / `extra_topk_length`. FlashMLA's interface allows reuse only while those values stay the same, but it asserts only shapes and config. Across the KV cache groups of one metadata build pass the values do match:\r\n- The SWA lengths come from the same query and sequence lengths, window, valid-token mask and `replay_start`. Each group builds the mask from its own slot mapping, but every sliding-window group writes each scheduled token, so the masks agree; `replay_start` is one tensor passed to every builder.\r\n- The top-k lengths count the valid entries each index layer writes. Every decode top-k path fills the entries past a row end derived from `seq_lens` and `next_n` alone with -1 (for example `rowEnd` in `csrc/libtorch_stable/sampler.cu`), under the same valid-token mask. So each index layer of a compress ratio yields `min(rowEnd, topk)`.\r\n\r\nThat second point is also why a group's same-type layers can already share one plan today, though they take their top-k from different index layers.\r\n\r\nThis PR lets the groups share the holders:\r\n- MRV2's `build_attn_metadata` passes one dict to every group's `CommonAttentionMetadata`, next to the existing `_token_to_req_indices_cache`.\r\n- The sliding-window builder takes its plan holders from that dict, keyed by its block size, causality and index width.\r\n\r\nWhat stays as before:\r\n- Callers that do not pass the dict (the V1 runner, the draft metadata refresh of the autoregressive speculator) keep one plan per group.\r\n- DBO micro-batches and the DSpark drafter build in separate passes, so they never share a plan.\r\n- SM100 does not take this path: it defaults to FlashMLA mega attention, which does not use these plan holders.\r\n\r\nPlanner launches per decode step drop from 21 to 4.\r\n\r\n**Duplicate check.** `gh pr list --repo vllm-project/vllm --state all --search` for `tile_sched`, `FlashMLASchedMeta`, `get_mla_metadata`, `tile scheduler` and `planner flashmla` shows no PR that shares the plan across KV cache groups.\r\n\r\nRelated: #58762 (RFC #58638) reuses Mamba/GDN metadata across KV cache groups in the same `build_attn_metadata` loop. This PR does the same for DeepSeek-V4's FlashMLA plans, which are built per group however many groups there are.\r\n\r\n## Test Plan\r\n\r\n**Setup**\r\n- Hardware: 8\u00d7 H20 (SM90, 96 GB, NVLink).\r\n- Model: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, HF revision `dba1be0a40aa45a94ad051997016db3960a90277`.\r\n- vLLM: \"main\" is the nightly wheel `0.30.1rc1.dev85+g8b84e1506` as installed; \"this PR\" is the same wheel with this diff applied as a Python overlay. The three changed files are identical between that nightly and current `main`.\r\n\r\n**Server.** TP8 + EP8. DSpark uses synthetic acceptance with a configured mean acceptance length of 3.51, the same distribution in both arms, so acceptance does not depend on the model's outputs:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nvllm deepseek_v41 --max-model-len 262144 \\\r\n  --max-num-batched-tokens 8192 --max-num-seqs 128 --gpu-memory-utilization 0.90 \\\r\n  --no-enable-prefix-caching --engram-config '{\"cpu_offload\":false}' "https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/41251", "title": "[Perf] Optimize DeepSeek V4.1 Flash Hopper paths and Blackwell prefill selection", "body": "## Motivation\n\nDeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on Hopper spends substantial decode time in group32 dense GEMMs, capacity-sized indexer work, mHC statistics, and padded Marlin MoE projections. This PR tunes those paths. On Blackwell, flattened prefill selection also launches separate mask, sort, slot-map and raw-offset operations; the added integer kernel combines them.\n\n## Changes\n\n- Tune Hopper group32 GEMMs and reuse exact BF16 expansions of eligible FP8 weights, preserving activation quantization boundaries and the original fallbacks.\n- Use live paged indexer lengths and variable-length top-k; fuse masking, sorting and slot mapping.\n- Enable guarded Hopper mHC combine/norm and compensated BF16 projection paths, with decode statistics overlap.\n- Preserve intermediate activation rounding in fused clipped SwiGLU; defer MXFP4 padding until Marlin repacking.\n- On SM100-family CUDA GPUs, fuse flattened prefill selection into one integer kernel. Keep the changing visible slot count as a non-specialized runtime argument, so new context lengths do not create new JIT variants. Hopper retains its existing Torch selection path.\n\n## Pinned validation\n\nCheckpoint `dba1be0a40aa45a94ad051997016db3960a90277`; PyTorch 2.13.0+cu130 and sgl-kernel 0.4.8; sgl-bench commit `a9da34ad1f997ca05878d858d3d01970e4a49af9`; sgl-eval 0.1.2. Both machines were allocated exclusively for these comparisons. All paired datasets, request counts, input/output lengths and API errors were validated from the request records. "github:sgl-project/sglang:40421", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/40421", "title": "[Perf] [NVIDIA] deepseek-v4.1: Reuse dequantized compressed KV in PD CP prefill (7/N)", "body": "issue:https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/39700\r\nbase pr: https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/40179\r\n\r\n## Summary\r\n\r\nDeepSeek V4.1 consumer layers can share the same compressed KV storage, but sparse prefill currently dequantizes that storage into a BF16 workspace on every layer.\r\n\r\nReuse the dequantized compressed KV within a forward to avoid repeated reads, dequantization, and workspace writes.\r\n<img width=\"2314\" height=\"610\" alt=\"image\" src=\"https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/528e5051-9a33-4982-b47a-cbc6bae0a527\" />\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n- Limit reuse to CP sparse BF16 prefill with compression ratios 1 and 2.\r\n- Keep separate workspaces per compression ratio.\r\n- Track validity by source layer and workspace address within the per-forward gather cache.\r\n- Refresh on producer execution, source changes, workspace reallocation, or a new forward.\r\n- Continue updating SWA and top-k inputs for every layer.\r\n\r\nThis retains additional scratch memory for the separate workspaces.\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\n- GPU regression tests cover shared sources, source updates, ratio interleaving, consecutive forwards, and workspace resizing.\r\n- All 16 short/long-context smoke-test outputs match the baseline.\r\n- In a six-step trace, compressed KV dequantization calls decrease from 228 to 24 (89.5% fewer).\r\n\r\n### Performance\r\nDeepSeek V4.1 Flash on 8\u00d7B200 with PD disaggregation; prefill uses TP4/EP4, CP, BCG, MegaMoE, and DSpark.
