- working config, measured throughput, rejected topologies", "body": "## Summary\n\nField data from running **DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash** as a production SGLang backend on **8\u00d7 RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q (SM120, 96 GB, PCIe-only \u2014 no NVLink)**, including the *only* topology we could get to start, measured prefill/decode, a speculative-decoding A/B, and the configuration traps that cost us real experiments. Posting because SM120 + V4.1-Flash users currently have to rediscover all of this from scratch.\n\nFull write-up (with methodology and reproduction artifacts): https://github.com/Lei-010/hermes-agent/blob/main/docs/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-SM120-Performance-and-Production-Config.md\n\nRelated: vLLM field report https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56700 (same hardware, vLLM side), vLLM https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56892 (SM120 bug/perf tracker).\n\n## What works\n\n```bash\ndocker run --gpus all -d --name dsv41 \\\n  --shm-size=64g --ipc=host --network host \\\n  --restart unless-stopped \\\n  -e TP_SIZE=8 -e DP_SIZE=1 -e EP_SIZE=8 \\\n  -e SPEC_ALGORITHM=dspark -e DSPARK_BLOCK_SIZE=6 \\\n  -e CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=2048 \\\n  -v /ssd/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash:/model \\\n  <sglang image>\n```\n\nEffective `server_args` (verified at runtime via `/get_server_info`):\n\n| Parameter | Value |\n|---|---|\n| `tp_size` / `ep_size` / `dp_size` | 8 / 8 / 1 |\n| `speculative_algorithm` | DSPARK, `speculative_dspark_block_size = 6` |\n| `chunked_prefill_size` | 2048 |\n| `context_length` | 1,048,576 |\n| `kv_cache_dtype` | `fp8_e4m3` |\n| `mem_fraction_static` | 0.72 |\n| `max_running_requests` | 16 |\n| `attention_backend` / `moe_runner_backend` | `dsv4` / `flashinfer_mxfp4` |\n| CUDA graph decode / prefill | full (max_bs 16) / **disabled** |\n\n**TP8/EP8 is not a preference \u2014 it is the only layout that starts** (see \"Topologies SGLang rejects\" below).\n\n## Measured throughput (cold, single stream, tokens from `usage`, not SSE chunk counts)\n\n128K cold prefill:\n\n| Config | tok/s |\n|---|---|\n| `chunked_prefill_size=2048` (production) | **6,853** |\n| same config re-run | 6,765 (\u22121.3%) |\n| flashmla prefill backend | 6,845 |\n| fused-MoE all-reduce | 6,828 |\n| `chunked_prefill_size=4096` | 6,301 |\n| `chunked_prefill_size=1024` | 6,012 |\n\n32K prompt: \u22487,196 tok/s. GPU util \u224887% at 220 W of 600 W \u2192 **communication-bound on PCIe**, not compute-bound. ~7K tok/s looks like the ceiling for this board without NVLink.\n\nRadix/prefix cache is the only large multiplier: cache-hit prefill **0.34 s for a 112K-token prefix \u2248 325K tok/s (\u224839\u00d7)**.\n\nDecode (client path over SSH tunnel, production config): prose 93.9 tok/s, structured/JSON 134.1 tok/s, TTFT 143\u2013169 ms.\n\n## DSPARK A/B (block_size=2, clean legs, each verified via `server_info` + acceptance length)\n\n| Prompt class | DSPARK ON | DSPARK OFF |\n|---|---|---|\n| prose | 76.5\u201378.9 tok/s | 36.4\u201336.5 tok/s |\n| structured | 120.3\u2013120.5 tok/s | 36.5 tok/s |\n| `accept len` | 3.0 tokens/step | 1.0 |\n\nWith DSPARK off the model flattens to ~36.5 tok/s regardless of prompt class \u2014 speculation is the difference between usable and unusable latency on this hardware. Production ships `block_size=6` (structured \u2248237\u2013252 tok/s vs 120 at block 2).\n\n## Traps that cost us experiments (SGLang-specific)\n\n1. **`--enable-expert-parallel` is a vLLM flag** \u2014 SGLang takes `ep_size`; the vLLM spelling is rejected outright.\n2. **`docker run` must pass `TP_SIZE`/`DP_SIZE`/`EP_SIZE` explicitly.** Without `-e`, the container silently serves TP8 defaults and your \"new\" config never happens (we recorded a full sweep of false data this way before adding a config gate).\n3. **`ep_size \u2264 tp_size`** or the MoE rank computation divides by zero: `moe_ep_rank = tp_rank % (tp_size // moe_dp_size) // (tp_size // moe_dp_size // ep_size)`.\n4. **DSPARK + DP attention requires `--enable-dp-lm-head`** \u2014 but is unreachable on this model anyway (next section).\n\n## Topologies SGLang rejects on this model\n\n| Attempt | Result |\n|---|---|\n| DP2 \u00d7 TP4 (any EP) | `ValueError: V4.1 vision currently supports TP/EP without DP, CP, PP or MoE A2A` \u2014 both replicas die within ~70 s |\n| prefill context-parallel | same class of rejection |\n| `--enable-dp-attention` alone | silently no DP group (`dp_size: 1`) \u2014 looks fine, isn't |\n\nOther SM120 gaps we hit: MSCCL++ (`_tune` kernel crash), FlashInfer all-reduce fusion, `flashmla_sparse_q8`, and CUDA-graph capture for **prefill** (crashes even with DSPARK off, i.e. unrelated to speculation).\n\n## Operational notes\n\n- A defunct container can survive a single `docker rm -f`; the next `docker run` then fails on the name conflict while an orphaned engine process keeps the port and answers health checks \u2014 which silently mislabels the next experiment. Loop `docker rm -f` until the container is gone **and** nothing listens on the port.\n- A parse failure must never satisfy an \"expected config\" assertion, or a server that has not started yet gets recorded as \"spec disabled\".\n- Verify intended config from the server (`/get_server_info`) before timing anything; This is the\n   first op of every layer, so a prompt prefilled alongside two others differs from\n   the same prompt prefilled alone in *every* token's logprob by ~1e-2. Pinned to the\n   token-independent `num_block_k // 4`. `mhc_fused_post_pre` carries the same term\n   plus a `num_tokens <= 32` FMA-kernel switch, and\n   `_flashinfer_mhc_pre_num_splits` is a third copy.\n2. **FlashMLA sparse decode tile scheduler** \u2014 `sparse_attn_decode_interface`\n   computes `payload = ceil(total_num_blocks / num_sm_parts)` over the whole batch,\n   so where a request's KV range is cut depends on its co-batched requests.\n   Deterministic mode issues one launch per request, which makes the schedule a\n   function of that request alone.\n3. **Non-paged indexer** \u2014 taken only when `batch_size == 1 and query_rows >= 8192`.\n4. **Router GEMM** \u2014 `MoEGate` switches to `dsv3_router_gemm` at\n   `hidden_states.shape[0] <= 16`, and `linear_bf16_fp32`'s HPC-Ops path at `M >= 8`.\n   Fixing the first also fixes a startup crash: the deterministic branch of `MoEGate`\n   returned bf16 router logits, and V4's fused `hash_topk` kernel requires fp32\n   (`Dtype value [bfloat16] not in the allowed options: [float32]`).\n\nAlso probed and ruled out on H200, so left alone: the DeepGEMM fp8 block GEMM is\nM-invariant at every V4 shape, `flash_mla_sparse_fwd` (prefill) is s_q-invariant,\nand the MoE runner choice makes no difference.\n\nDeterministic inference on `dsv4` is rejected on SM120, whose attention kernel is an\nautotuned Triton kernel that picks its tile size \u2014 and with it the softmax rescaling\norder \u2014 from measured timings.\n\nEverything above is gated: with the flag off, the only changes are an extracted\n`_use_sparse_prefill` predicate, two algebraically identical `max_splits` locals and\na `use_fma` local. Verified below.\n\n## Accuracy Tests\n\nDeepSeek-V4-Flash-FP8, 4xH200, TP=4.\n\n**The feature.** Distinct outputs over 32 batch sizes,\n`sglang.test.test_deterministic --test-mode single`:\n\n| | distinct outputs / 32 |\n| --- | --- |\n| without the flag | 6 |\n| with `--enable-deterministic-inference` | **1** |\n\nAll three harness modes pass with radix cache and CUDA graphs on: `single` (n=32),\n`prefix` + logprobs (temperature 0.5, shared prefixes of 1 / 511 / 2048 / 4097), and\n`radix_cache`. Chunked prefill was checked separately at\n`--chunked-prefill-size 512` over 4097-token prompts.\n\n**No regression with the flag off.** Same box, arms run back to back, benchmark client always from `main` so only the\nserver tree varies.\n\nThe strongest check pins the batch at one request, which removes batch-composition\nnoise entirely: 150 GSM8K completions issued serially are **150/150 byte-identical**\nbetween `main` and this branch, as are 8/8 fixed greedy prompts.\n\nGSM8K, 400 questions, 5-shot, greedy, `--parallel 64`:\n\n| tree | runs |\n| --- | --- |\n| `main` | 0.965, 0.965, 0.968, 0.968, 0.968 |\n| this branch | 0.960, 0.963, 0.970, 0.960 |\n\nRepeats within one warm server span 0.960-0.970 on this branch, bracketing every\n`main` number; at 400 questions the harness cannot resolve better than about +/-4\nquestions. That spread is DeepSeek-V4's own batch-composition non-determinism -- the\nthing the new flag removes.\n\n## Speed Tests and Profiling\n\n**With the flag off** \u2014 `sglang.benchmark.serving`, random 1024/512, 200 prompts,\nconcurrency 32: \n\n| order | tree | output tok/s | total tok/s | mean TPOT |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| 1st | `main` | 1849.80 | 5549.39 | 14.01 ms |\n| 2nd | this branch | 1919.93 | 5759.80 | 13.99 ms |\n| 3rd | `main` | 1919.67 | 5759.01 | 13.99 ms |\n\nThis branch lands within 0.01% of the warm `main` run. Recent works like FuseLLM and FuseChat have demonstrated\nthe potential of transferring multiple model capabilities to lightweight\nmodels, enhancing adaptability and efficiency, which motivates our\ninvestigation into more efficient cross-capability transfer methods. We show this recompute is avoidable, and identify exactly what naive KV reuse loses: the cross-chunk conditioning a chunk absorbs from its neighbours. This loss is asymmetric. The direct readout of a cached chunk is recovered exactly and for free by the standard state-merge. What remains is a diffuse, low-rank residue concentrated in deep layers, invisible to single-hop retrieval but precisely what multi-hop reasoning binds on. Blind reuse therefore leaves single-hop recall intact while halving multi-hop accuracy; this is the failure mode prior position-independent caches, designed for single-context or single-image reuse, do not address. We repair it with a small, training-free low-rank conditioning patch stored alongside each position-free chunk. Reuse reduces to one operator across MLA, GQA, and MHA: exact RoPE re-rotation to any target position, plus the patch that restores cross-chunk binding. This makes three window operations cheap: reorder (one patch serves every ordering of a cached set), sliding-window survival (surviving chunks relocate via rotation only, zero re-encode), and recall (an evicted chunk is rehydrated by its patch, never re-encoded). A rank-m patch recovers full task accuracy on cross-chunk-binding benchmarks, MM-NIAH across two attention families and two-page doc-QA, at a fraction of the KV footprint, and reconstructs re-prefill KV to within bf16 rounding in a production SGLang kernel across six backbones. "metadata": {"number": 3501, "labels": [], "comments": 0, "state_reason": null}, "content_hash": "ce7042557c59c8f98e8a5fb01f38e0ab1f5f2af33515ebf9c27b41e09d90e8d8", "rank": -4.535054107676491}, {"id": "github:SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX:4104", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/pull/4104", "title": "feat(matrix): budget decode GPUs for P/D-shared DRAM offload pools / feat(matrix): \u4e3a P/D \u5171\u4eab\u7684 DRAM \u5378\u8f7d\u6c60\u8ba1\u5165\u89e3\u7801 GPU \u9884\u7b97", "body": "Disaggregated AgentX entries budget `total-cpu-dram-gb` from one prefill replica's GPUs on its node, so DRAM beside the decode workers is never counted, even when the KV offload pool lives there. This PR adds an opt-in `dram-budget-scope: all-workers` for a pool shared by prefill and decode. Every prefill and decode GPU claims its node share, the same GPUs that normalize per-GPU throughput. The default stays `prefill`, and no config, recipe or benchmark point changes here. Closes #4103.\n\n### What changes\n\n- `AgenticCodingConfig` gains `dram-budget-scope: prefill | all-workers`, default `prefill`. `all-workers` requires `prefill` and `decode` workers and rejects per-worker `hardware`, because the budget reads one runner's node DRAM.\n- Under `all-workers`, `agentic_dram_offload_gb` counts `num-worker \u00d7 tp \u00d7 pp \u00d7 pcp-size \u00d7 dwdp-size` for both roles, with the same node-tiling rules as today. The per-GPU share is unchanged: capped node DRAM \u00d7 `dram-utilization` / `gpus-per-node`.\n- `configs/CONFIGS.md` documents `dram-utilization` and the new field. The total may span the deployment's nodes, but each node holds at most the share of the deployment's GPUs on it, so idle GPUs claim nothing. The budget caps what a recipe may use; nodes still need DRAM for hugepage reservations, page cache and processes.\n\n| 8-GPU nodes, `dram-utilization: 1.0` | GPUs | `prefill` (default) | `all-workers` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1P1D: 4-GPU prefill + 4-GPU decode | 8 | 1,499 GB | 2,999 GB |\n| 1P2D | 12 | 1,499 GB | 4,499 GB |\n| 1P3D | 16 | 1,499 GB | 5,999 GB |\n\n### Our design: P/D with a Mooncake Store over RDMA\n\nOur GLM-5.2 MI355X points (#4045) run ATOM with a Mooncake Store as prefill's second-tier prefix cache, implemented in [ROCm/ATOM#2464](https://github.com/ROCm/ATOM/pull/2464).\n\n- **Roles.** Prefill is one ATOM worker with PP4 \u00d7 TP1: four pipeline stages, one per GPU, and each GPU uses its own RDMA NIC. Decode is one ATOM worker with TP4 and DCP4. atomesh routes requests.\n- **Cache tiers.** Prefill keeps a paged KV prefix cache in HBM (L1). The Mooncake Store (L2) is a DRAM pool: masters hold object metadata, leases and LRU eviction, and owner processes pin hugepage DRAM and register it with an RDMA NIC. Owners lend memory, not compute; the NICs move the bytes.\n- **KV moves over RDMA**, numbered as in the diagram below:\n  1. **Lookup.** On admission, the prefill scheduler asks the masters over TCP which 256-token chunks every PP stage holds. The hit is the shortest prefix all stages hold. Only metadata moves.\n  2. **Put.** As prefill computes, each stage packs its new chunks in place into a Store-registered slot pool in HBM. Its NIC writes them into an owner's DRAM (`batch_put_from`, GPUDirect RDMA).\n  3. **Get.** On a hit, the NIC reads the chunks from whichever node's owner holds them straight into the slot pool (`batch_get_into`). The stage unpacks them into the paged KV instead of recomputing them.\n  4. **P/D.** Finished KV reaches the decode GPUs over the mooncake P/D connector, also by RDMA. The decode path never reads the Store.\n  5. **Decode-side DRAM.** Owners also run beside decode, on the decode node or on the decode half of a shared node, and mount on the same masters. Their DRAM joins the pool that prefill reads and writes over RDMA, at no cost to the decode GPUs. In ATOM's two-node test they held 836 of their 960 GiB at the end of a c96 run.\n- **Rails.** A stage on `rdmaN` uses pool N, whose owners on every node use `rdmaN`. This fits rail-isolated fabrics such as `mi355x-amds`.\n\nAt AgentX c112, this pool's capacity sets throughput. Conversations that idle for the trace's 300 s cap return just as a 1,499 GB Store evicts them, and prefill must recompute their prefixes. #4103 has the measurements.\n\n### Design diagram\n\nFrom ROCm/ATOM#2464. It shows a two-node cell; in a single-node 1P1D, the node's two NUMA halves play node A and node B.\n\n![mooncake_store: one Mooncake Store pool shared across nodes and P/D instances](https://github.com/ROCm/ATOM/blob/61ac476f998d818a2a1f27fc0bd1475d401a90ca/pr2464/mooncake_store_architecture.svg?raw=true)\n\n<details>\n<summary>\u4e2d\u6587</summary>\n\n\u5206\u79bb\u5f0f GitHub", "body": "Conversation\nChtholly-Boss\ncommented\nSep 18, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nSummary\nReplace\nfmaxf(x, 0)\nwith\nx + fabsf(x)\nin the SM90 FP8 MQA logits epilogue, recovering the 2\u00d7 throughput gap between FMNMX (half-rate) and FADD (full-rate) on Hopper. The factor-of-2 introduced by the identity is resolved by folding\n0.5f\ninto the existing\nscale_kv\nmultiply \u2014 zero extra instructions, bit-exact numerics.\nBackground\nOn SM90,\nfmaxf(x, 0)\ncompiles to a single FMNMX, which issues at 64 ops/clk/SM \u2014 half the rate of FADD/FFMA (128 ops/clk/SM). Meanwhile\nx + |x|\ncompiles to a single FADD with the absolute-value operand modifier, which is a free encoding bit in the instruction word:\nFADD R3, R3, |R3|    ; one instruction, full rate (cuobjdump, sm_90a)\nThe relu executes once per\n(token, head, kv)\nscore element in the MQA logits epilogue, so at 64 vs 128 ops/clk/SM the FMNMX form doubles the FP32 pipe time spent on relu alone.\nSince\nx + |x| = 2 \u00b7 relu(x)\nholds exactly for all finite IEEE-754 floats (\n+0\nfor x \u2264 0), the doubled sum is simply halved by folding\n* 0.5f\ninto the already-existing\nscale_kv\nmultiplication (an exponent decrement, also exact):\n//\nBefore:\nreturn\nfmaxf(shifted_accum[j],\n0\n) * weights[i][(j /\n4\n) *\n2\n+ (j &\n1\n)];\n...\nfloat\nv_0 = (sum[\n0\n] + sum[\n1\n]) * scale_kv_0;\n//\nAfter:\nreturn\n(shifted_accum[j] + fabsf(shifted_accum[j])) * weights[i][(j /\n4\n) *\n2\n+ (j &\n1\n)];\n...\nfloat\nv_0 = (sum[\n0\n] + sum[\n1\n]) * (scale_kv_0 *\n0\n.\n5f\n);\nThe same technique is already used by the SM100 kernel (\nsm100_mqa_logits.cuh\n, packed\nFADD2\nvariant); this PR brings it to SM90. Verified via\ncuobjdump -sass\n: zero FMNMX across all JIT-instantiated\nsm90_fp8_mqa_logits\ncubins.\nPerformance (H200, 132 SMs, SM90A)\nProduction indexer shapes, best of 3 reps, before \u2192 after:\nShape\nBefore\nAfter\nGain\nH=32, D=128, SQ=512, SKV=140k, FP32 logits\n1018 TFLOPS\n1093 TFLOPS\n+7.4%\nSQ=512, SKV=130560, H=64, BF16, CP=1\n1042 TFLOPS\n1126 TFLOPS\n+8.1%\nSQ=512, SKV=130560, H=32, BF16, CP=0\n941 TFLOPS\n989 TFLOPS\n+5.1%\nSQ=2048, SKV=8192, H=64, BF16, CP=1\n980 TFLOPS\n1027 TFLOPS\n+4.8%\nFull\ntests/test_attention.py\nsweep (192 contiguous MQA cases on SM90):\navg +3.7%\n(SK=65536 subset +4.4%, H=64 subset +5.5%, best case +11.3%). Gains scale with the relu's share of kernel time: larger SKV, more heads, smaller head dim \u2192 bigger win. Paged MQA logits (separate kernel, untouched) is unaffected.\nCorrectness\nx + |x| = 2 \u00b7 relu(x)\nexactly for all finite IEEE-754 floats (x > 0:\nx + x = 2x\n; x \u2264 0:\nx + (\u2212x) = +0\n); the \u00f72 via\n* 0.5f\nis an exact exponent decrement.\nAll 192 SM90 cases in\ntests/test_attention.py\npass (numeric diff vs simulated reference, 20\u00d7 bitwise self-consistency, mask equality) \u2014 verified on H200.\nNaN semantics differ (\nx + |x|\npropagates NaN;\nfmaxf(NaN, 0)\nreturns 0), but WGMMA accumulator outputs are finite in practice; this matches the SM100 kernel's existing behavior.\nChanges\ndeep_gemm/include/deep_gemm/impls/sm90_fp8_mqa_logits.cuh\n\u2014 3 lines modified (transform lambda + two\n* 0.5f\nfolds).\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nUse full-rate FADD relu in SM90 FP8 MQA logits epilogue\n\u2026\nee53a16\nFMNMX (fmaxf(x, 0)) issues at 64 ops/clk/SM on SM90, half the rate of\nFADD/FFMA (128 ops/clk/SM), while the abs operand modifier is a free\nencoding bit on FADD. Since x + |x| == 2 * relu(x) exactly for finite\nfloats, the compensating 0.5f is folded into the existing scale_kv\nmultiply: zero extra instructions, bit-exact numerics.\n\nNaN semantics differ (x + |x| propagates NaN; fmaxf(NaN, 0) returns 0),\nmatching the existing SM100 kernel (sm100_mqa_logits.cuh).\n\nMeasured on H200: +7.4% on the production indexer shape\n(H=32, D=128, SQ=512, SKV=140k), avg +3.7% over the 192-case\ntest_attention.py sweep.\nChtholly-Boss\nchanged the title\nUse full-rate FADD relu in SM90 FP8 MQA logits epilogue\nperf: Use full-rate FADD relu in SM90 FP8 MQA logits epilogue\nSep 18, 2026\nds-review-bot\nreviewed\nSep 18, 2026\nView reviewed changes\nComment thread\ndeep_gemm/include/deep_gemm/impls/sm90_fp8_mqa_logits.cuh\nauto shifted_accum = accum + i * kNumAccumPerReduce;\nconst auto transform = [&](const uint32_t& j) {\nreturn\nfmaxf\n(shifted_accum[j]\n, 0\n) * weights[i][(j / 4) * 2 + (j & 1)];\nreturn (shifted_accum[j]\n+ fabsf(shifted_accum[j])\n) * weights[i][(j / 4) * 2 + (j & 1)];\nds-review-bot\nSep 18, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nCollaborator\nThere was a problem hiding this comment.\nChoose a reason for hiding this comment\nThe reason will be displayed to describe this comment to others.\nLearn more\n.\nChoose a reason\nSpam\nAbuse\nOff Topic\nOutdated\nDuplicate\nResolved\nLow Quality\nHide\ncomment\n\ud83d\udd35\nsuggestion\n: Non-blocking:\nshifted_accum[j] + fabsf(shifted_accum[j])\nis a deliberately non-obvious spelling of\n2 * relu(x)\n, and the compensating\n0.5f\nlives ~12 lines away inside the\nscale_kv\nmultiply. This adds CuTe DSL kernels plus an `_sm90_dispatch.py` router, wired into the existing public entry points behind `is_sm90a_supported()`.\r\n\r\nMotivation is MiniMax-M3 serving on H100, where the MSA path otherwise falls back to Triton.\r\n\r\n**Scope.** \r\nSM90 implements the *serving* surface rather than the full SM12x surface, and every narrowing raises instead of silently returning wrong numbers:\r\n\r\n| | SM90  Support Surface|\r\n|---|---|\r\n| KV layout | paged only (`page_table` + `seqused_k`); ragged varlen raises |\r\n| K/V allocation | must be interleaved in one buffer (`v` is the second half of `k`'s last dim) |\r\n| KV dtype | fp8 e4m3 only |\r\n| `topk` | 16 only |\r\n| LSE / temperature LSE | not returned |\r\n| per-tensor `k_scale`/`v_scale`/`k_global_scale` | not supported (`softmax_scale` and `v_global_scale` *are*, folded into q and the output) |\r\n| indexer (proxy score) | fp8 : prefill and decode; bf16 :decode only, prefill support to be added later |\r\n\r\nThe packed-KV and paged requirements are free on the serving path \u2014 vLLM already stores MSA K and V interleaved in one paged allocation and hands out the halves, so the view is a reinterpretation, not a copy. Materializing either otherwise would copy the whole cache per call, so the dispatcher refuses instead.\r\n\r\nSparse prefill routes on `max_pages_per_seq`: a per-CTA page-union kernel at or below 800, and a one-query-token-per-CTA kernel above it. The union kernel's tile budget is correct only up to mpg 960 (measured: OK \u2264 960, wrong \u2265 1024), so 800 keeps margin. All four ops are CUDA-graph capturable, including across multiple captured batch sizes.\r\n\r\n**Preliminary end-to-end numbers.**\r\nMiniMax-M3 MXFP8, TP8 on 8\u00d7H100 80GB, vLLM\r\nv0.27.1 with EAGLE3 (K=3) speculative decoding, agentic-coding trace replay.\r\nBoth arms are the same server configuration and differ *only* in which kernels\r\nserve MSA \u2014 same node, same FlashInfer build, same flags, same KV cache dtype:\r\n\r\n| concurrency | ITL (Triton MSA) | ITL (FlashInfer MSA) | \u0394 | output tok/s (Triton) | output tok/s (FlashInfer) | \u0394 |\r\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\r\n| 1 | 73.6 ms | 33.0 ms | **\u221255.2%** | 24.9 | 35.0 | **+40.5%** |\r\n| 2 | 82.4 ms | 36.0 ms | **\u221256.3%** | 25.8 | 48.1 | **+86.8%** |\r\n| 4 | 142.2 ms | 66.6 ms | **\u221253.2%** | 44.0 | 67.8 | **+54.1%** |\r\n| 5 | 134.9 ms | 57.4 ms | **\u221257.5%** | 40.9 | 82.1 | **+100.9%** |\r\n\r\nGeometric mean: **ITL \u221256%**, **output throughput +69%** (ITL p50: \u221261%).\r\n\r\nBoth arms pin `--attention-backend TRITON_ATTN`, so the dense attention layers (3 of 60) are identical and the delta comes from the 57 MSA layers only; the arms differ by a single environment switch on one node. ROCm is referenced only as prior art \u2014 the bot CC'd ROCm maintainers on keywords, apologies for the noise.\nDeepSeek-V4.1's two-level indexer publishes candidate blocks at\ncandidate_source_layer_id\n; later consumer indexers are meant to select their top-k\ninside those candidate blocks only\n. On SM100 this is implemented with DeepGEMM's sparse MQA-logits kernels (\nfp8_fp4_(paged_)sparse_mqa_logits\n). On SM90 there is no equivalent, so the consumers fall back to dense scoring over the full context followed by a mask.\nvLLM currently implements candidate-consuming indexers in one of two ways:\nSM100\n\u2014\nfp8_fp4_sparse_mqa_logits\n/\nfp8_fp4_paged_sparse_mqa_logits\nvia\nDeepseekV41SparseIndexerBackend\n. Opt-in through\nAttentionConfig.indexer_sparse_logits\nand hard-gated on SM100-class hardware (\nvllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/sparse_indexer.py\n, the\nis_device_capability_family(100)\ncheck), the MXFP4 indexer cache, DeepGEMM >= 2.8, and the DeepSelect extension.\nROCm (gfx950)\n\u2014 candidate-only MQA scorers reading the candidate pool in place, in review as\n[ROCm][Perf] Candidate-only MQA scorer for DSA sparse indexer prefill on gfx950\n#57858\n(prefill) and\n[ROCm][Perf] Candidate-only paged MQA scorer for DSA sparse indexer decode on gfx950\n#57859\n(decode).\nSM90 (H20)\n\u2014 neither. The consumers run the full-width\nfp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits\nin\nvllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_attn_indexer.py\nand then mask the scores down to the candidate blocks with\n_apply_candidate_mask\n(\nvllm/model_executor/kernels/attention/dsa/candidate_blocks.py\n). The candidate mechanism keeps the semantics but not the compute saving.\nMeasured on H20 (SM90), TP8, 1M context, decode, FP8 indexer cache, torch profiler:\nConcurrency\nPer consumer layer\n4 consumer layers\nShare of a step\n~10\n2.067 ms\n8.28 ms\n20% of 40.75 ms\n1\n196 \u00b5s\n0.78 ms\n6%\nLayers 24/28/32/36 each make one full-context\nfp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits\ncall per step. With\ncandidate_topk_blocks\n= 2048 and\ncandidate_block_size\n= 8, the candidate set is 16 384 positions against a 1 048 576-position compressed context \u2014\n1.6% of the scored columns\n\u2014 so roughly 98% of each consumer's scoring work is discarded by the mask. The cost scales linearly with both concurrency and context length.\nThe requested feature is a CUDA/SM90 way to score only the candidate blocks.\nThe MXFP4 coupling blocks SM90 today\nindexer_sparse_logits\ncannot be enabled on SM90 at all, and not only because of the GPU-family check. The flag also requires the MXFP4 indexer cache (\nsparse_indexer.py\n, and again in\nmodels/deepseek_v41/attention.py\n), and MXFP4 itself is rejected before that:\n# vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/indexer.py\nif\nuse_fp4\nand\nnot\ncurrent_platform\n.\nis_device_capability_family\n(\n100\n):\nraise\nValueError\n(\n\"indexer_kv_dtype='mxfp4' requires Blackwell datacenter GPUs \"\n\"(sm_10x, e.g. B200/GB200); sm_120 (consumer Blackwell) and \"\n\"earlier architectures are not supported.\"\n)\nHopper has no FP4 datapath, so SM90 necessarily runs the FP8 indexer cache (the\nauto\ndefault, one float32 scale per token). MXFP4 is an input requirement of the SM100 DeepGEMM kernels, not part of the \"score only the candidate blocks\" semantics \u2014 the ROCm path, for instance, scores the candidate pool with fp32 logits. So enabling candidate-only scoring on SM90 means letting that semantic flag be satisfied by an FP8 implementation.\nQuestion for maintainers:\nshould candidate-only scoring on SM90 reuse\nindexer_sparse_logits\n(relaxing its MXFP4 requirement to a per-platform one), or should it be a separate opt-in with its own builder type? The former keeps one user-facing knob and makes a future SM100 migration a no-op; the latter keeps each platform's preconditions self-contained.\nAlternatives\nA. Gather + reuse the existing dense kernel (preferred).\nConsumers gather their candidate K from the index cache (~2 MB per request) and score the gathered candidate set with the existing dense logits kernel, dropping the mask pass. No new scoring kernel. The scored K set and the query are identical to the dense-then-mask path, so the resulting logits should be bit-identical. A relevant primitive already exists on CUDA:\ncp_gather_indexer_k_quant_cache\n(\ntorch.ops._C_cache_ops\n, added in\nAdd gather_indexer_k_quant_cache kernel\n#25931\n/\n[Deepseek-V3.2][Kernel] Integrate cuda indexer k cache gather\n#26456\n) gathers paged indexer K \u2014 FP8 values plus the 4-byte scale \u2014 into a contiguous workspace, and the prefill path uses it today. It gathers contiguous ", "created_at": null, "updated_at": null, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:15:39Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:vllm-project/vllm:61830", "tags": ["b200", "h100", "deepseek-v4.1-flash", "deepseek", "mla", "tpot", "throughput", "performance", "speculation", "nvfp4"], "metadata": {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:61830", "repo": "vllm-project/vllm", "number": 61830, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:15:36Z", "discussion_coverage": "HTML-visible only; not exhaustive formal reviews or paginated replies", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/61830", "html_sha256": "aef7d22f461b8467e2259caf733ed62d64fd85dfc0c7fc966574813b3730e594", "text_sha256": "b2f61c539191730d435419b335db2df4ff0bddbbe58a326999bcaa928f0106c5", "characters": 10173, "title": "[Feature]: Candidate-only scoring for DeepSeek-V4.1 indexer consumers on SM90 (H20) \u00b7 Issue #61830 \u00b7 vllm-project/vllm \u00b7 GitHub"}, "content_hash": "28983e0bf5b1f41bfcbefd7edffeb596cf932f6942d23d80cc6f1556efb3f33c", "rank": -26.690227426207393}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/vllm:62000", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62000", "title": "[Perf][Attention] Candidate-only FP8 indexer decode scoring on SM90", "body": "\n\n> **Status: draft.** Opened early to get direction feedback before investing in\n> the long-term version. The optimization itself works and is measured; the\n> path-selection policy is deliberately provisional and will be reworked (see\n> [Roadmap](#roadmap)). Comments on the approach are more useful than comments\n> on the gating details, which are expected to change.\n\n## Overview\n\nOn SM90 the DSv4.1 candidate-consuming indexers have no paged sparse scoring\nkernel, so they score the entire compressed context per decode row and then\nmask down to the candidate blocks. This adds an opt-in path that gathers the\ncandidate K from the paged FP8 indexer cache and scores only the candidates.\nImplements the alternative-A direction of #61830.\n\nNot a duplicate: #57858 and #57859 implement candidate-only scoring for ROCm\n(gfx950); this is the SM90/CUDA counterpart called for in #61830.\n\n## Claims\n\nClaiming only what is measured at this stage:\n\n- At ~1M context, 9 concurrent requests on 8x H20 (TP8): the four\n  candidate-consuming indexer layers drop from **9.57 ms to 3.05 ms** per step,\n  and per-step time from **39.04 ms to 31.53 ms** (profiler-derived).\n- Selected top-k sets are equivalent to the existing dense score-then-mask\n  path.\n- Default behaviour is unchanged: the feature is off by default, and\n  unsupported shapes or platforms fall back to dense rather than failing.\n- Known cost: a single-request step is about **11.6% slower** than dense, since\n  the candidate path carries a per-step fixed cost. The provisional workload\n  gates exist to avoid that region until a better policy lands.\n\n## Validation\n\nSetup: 8x H20, TP8, DSv4.1, `num_speculative_tokens=5`, ~1M token context.\nWith `1 + 5 = 6` tokens per request the SM90 paged kernel takes the flatten\npath, so one active request contributes roughly 6 decode rows.\n\n**Kernel / unit**\n\n```bash\npytest tests/kernels/attention/test_candidate_gather_cudagraph.py\npytest tests/model_executor/layers/test_mla_short_prefill_indexer.py\n```\n\n- `test_candidate_gather_cudagraph.py` (new): gather + scoring + remap under\n  CUDA graph capture and replay.\n- `test_mla_short_prefill_indexer.py` (extended): dense vs. candidate-only\n  equivalence of the selected top-k set.\n\n**Equivalence**\n\nWith `VLLM_DSA_CANDIDATE_GATHER_FORCE=1`, the selected top-k index set matches\nthe dense path on identical inputs. Note that #61830's alternative-A hoped for\nbit-identical results; this verifies set equivalence instead, since the\ngather-then-score path uses a different scoring kernel and top-k tie-breaking\ncan differ (documented in the module docstring).\n\n**Server-level**\n\nReal `vllm serve` at ~1M context, torch profiler traces collected with the\nfeature off and on:\n\n| Configuration | Per step | Four consumer indexer layers |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Dense (feature off) | 39.04 ms | 9.57 ms |\n| Candidate-only (feature on) | 31.53 ms | 3.05 ms |\n\nat 9 concurrent requests (~54 decode rows).\n\nTo reproduce, enable on an SM90 FP8-cache deployment:\n\n```bash\nexport VLLM_DSA_CANDIDATE_GATHER_DECODE=1\nexport VLLM_DSA_CANDIDATE_GATHER_MIN_ROWS=16\n```\n\n**Validation still in progress** \u2014 explicit about what the numbers above do\n*not* yet cover, and queued in this order:\n\n1. A profiler-free end-to-end throughput/latency A/B (current timings are\n   profiler-derived).\n2. Model-level accuracy evaluation plus an independent numerical reference\n   (correctness evidence so far is top-k set equivalence against vLLM's own\n   dense path).\n3. Cross-hardware validation on SM90 parts other than H20 \u2014 the reason no\n   crossover constant is hardcoded.\n\nI will keep appending results to this PR as they come in.\n\n## Details\n\n**Why the dense path is wasteful here.** The candidate-consuming indexers are\nhanded a small, fixed-width candidate set, but SM90 lacks a paged sparse\nscoring kernel, so they score the whole context and discard everything outside\nthe candidate blocks. The discarded work grows linearly with context length\n(~98% of the scoring work at 1M context, per #61830).\n\n**What the new path does.**\n\n1. Gather each candidate block's K from the paged FP8 indexer cache into a\n   compact per-row buffer.\n2. Score only the gathered candidates with the existing non-paged FP8 scoring\n   kernel.\n3. Remap the selected columns back to request-local token positions, so the\n   output is identical in meaning to the dense path.\n\n**Scope kept narrow on purpose.** Only the candidate-consuming indexers take\nthe new path; the candidate-source and pre-candidate indexers stay dense, as\nthey have no candidate set to exploit. Restricted to SM90 with an FP8 indexer\ncache and no context parallelism \u2014 SM100 already has native sparse kernels,\nand all other platforms keep today's behaviour.\n\n**Tradeoff, and why the current gating is provisional.** Dense cost scales\nwith `rows x context`, while candidate scoring is dominated by a per-step\nfixed cost, so the crossover tracks scanned work relative to the candidate\nwidth rather than context length alone. ROCm is referenced only as prior art \u2014 the bot CC'd ROCm maintainers on keywords, apologies for the noise.\n\nDeepSeek-V4.1's two-level indexer publishes candidate blocks at `candidate_source_layer_id`; later consumer indexers are meant to select their top-k **inside those candidate blocks only**. On SM100 this is implemented with DeepGEMM's sparse MQA-logits kernels (`fp8_fp4_(paged_)sparse_mqa_logits`). On SM90 there is no equivalent, so the consumers fall back to dense scoring over the full context followed by a mask.\n\nvLLM currently implements candidate-consuming indexers in one of two ways:\n\n1. **SM100** \u2014 `fp8_fp4_sparse_mqa_logits` / `fp8_fp4_paged_sparse_mqa_logits` via `DeepseekV41SparseIndexerBackend`. Opt-in through `AttentionConfig.indexer_sparse_logits` and hard-gated on SM100-class hardware (`vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/sparse_indexer.py`, the `is_device_capability_family(100)` check), the MXFP4 indexer cache, DeepGEMM >= 2.8, and the DeepSelect extension.\n2. **ROCm (gfx950)** \u2014 candidate-only MQA scorers reading the candidate pool in place, in review as #57858 (prefill) and #57859 (decode).\n3. **SM90 (H20)** \u2014 neither. The consumers run the full-width `fp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits` in `vllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_attn_indexer.py` and then mask the scores down to the candidate blocks with `_apply_candidate_mask` (`vllm/model_executor/kernels/attention/dsa/candidate_blocks.py`). The candidate mechanism keeps the semantics but not the compute saving.\n\nMeasured on H20 (SM90), TP8, 1M context, decode, FP8 indexer cache, torch profiler:\n\n| Concurrency | Per consumer layer | 4 consumer layers | Share of a step |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| ~10 | 2.067 ms | 8.28 ms | 20% of 40.75 ms |\n| 1 | 196 \u00b5s | 0.78 ms | 6% |\n\nLayers 24/28/32/36 each make one full-context `fp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits` call per step. With `candidate_topk_blocks` = 2048 and `candidate_block_size` = 8, the candidate set is 16 384 positions against a 1 048 576-position compressed context \u2014 **1.6% of the scored columns** \u2014 so roughly 98% of each consumer's scoring work is discarded by the mask. The cost scales linearly with both concurrency and context length.\n\nThe requested feature is a CUDA/SM90 way to score only the candidate blocks.\n\n### The MXFP4 coupling blocks SM90 today\n\n`indexer_sparse_logits` cannot be enabled on SM90 at all, and not only because of the GPU-family check. The flag also requires the MXFP4 indexer cache (`sparse_indexer.py`, and again in `models/deepseek_v41/attention.py`), and MXFP4 itself is rejected before that:\n\n```python\n# vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/indexer.py\nif use_fp4 and not current_platform.is_device_capability_family(100):\n    raise ValueError(\n        \"indexer_kv_dtype='mxfp4' requires Blackwell datacenter GPUs \"\n        \"(sm_10x, e.g. B200/GB200); sm_120 (consumer Blackwell) and \"\n        \"earlier architectures are not supported.\"\n    )\n```\n\nHopper has no FP4 datapath, so SM90 necessarily runs the FP8 indexer cache (the `auto` default, one float32 scale per token). MXFP4 is an input requirement of the SM100 DeepGEMM kernels, not part of the \"score only the candidate blocks\" semantics \u2014 the ROCm path, for instance, scores the candidate pool with fp32 logits. So enabling candidate-only scoring on SM90 means letting that semantic flag be satisfied by an FP8 implementation.\n\n**Question for maintainers:** should candidate-only scoring on SM90 reuse `indexer_sparse_logits` (relaxing its MXFP4 requirement to a per-platform one), or should it be a separate opt-in with its own builder type? The former keeps one user-facing knob and makes a future SM100 migration a no-op; the latter keeps each platform's preconditions self-contained.\n\n### Alternatives\n\n- **A. Gather + reuse the existing dense kernel (preferred).** Consumers gather their candidate K from the index cache (~2 MB per request) and score the gathered candidate set with the existing dense logits kernel, dropping the mask pass. No new scoring kernel. The scored K set and the query are identical to the dense-then-mask path, so the resulting logits should be bit-identical. A relevant primitive already exists on CUDA: `cp_gather_indexer_k_quant_cache` (`torch.ops._C_cache_ops`, added in #25931 / #26456) gathers paged indexer K \u2014 FP8 values plus the 4-byte scale \u2014 into a contiguous workspace, and the prefill path uses it today. It gathers contiguous ranges addressed by `cu_seq_lens`, so it is a starting point rather than a drop-in: candidate-block gathering needs scattered block IDs.\n- **B. Native SM90 sparse MQA-logits kernel.** Mirrors the SM100 DeepGEMM semantics with a new SM90 kernel, removing the gather hop. Better ceiling, substantially more implementation and validation cost.\n- **C. Two-level selection inside the consumer (HISA-style).** Block-level coarse pass plus a refined pass inside the candidates. "source": "huggingface", "kind": "paper", "url": "https://huggingface.co/papers/2610.13990", "title": "DoSA: Dots Sparse Attention", "body": "Sparse attention visits few keys, yet finding them can still require scoring every key with every indexer head. We introduce DoSA (Dots Sparse Attention), a lossless method for making this computation conditional while preserving exact token selection. Its central observation is that Top-K selection requires sufficient evidence to reject a candidate, not necessarily its full score. A seed set supplies a lower bound on the winning threshold. Nonnegative head projections and joint residual bounds upper-bound positive contributions, while activation-informed aggregation bounds the magnitude subtracted by negative heads. The certificate directs full scoring toward candidates that remain eligible; exact-score filtering further reduces writeback and selection work. We establish the certificate and its exact-selection conditions, and analyze how geometry and selection margins govern its effectiveness. Our GPU implementation accelerates the complete Indexer by up to 2.74 times over SGLang and 1.69 times over LiteTopK across English, Chinese, and code workloads. End-to-end evaluation on GLM-5.2 and DeepSeek-V3.2 achieves up to 1.94 times and 1.50 times time-to-first-token speedups over the two baselines, respectively.", "created_at": "2026-09-26T00:00:00.000Z", "updated_at": "2026-09-26T00:00:00.000Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:30:17Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["deepseek"], "metadata": {"read_depth": "abstract", "query": "candidate-only paged indexer scoring", "githubRepo": "https://github.com/llm-sys/flash-indexer", "arxiv_id": "2610.13990"}, "content_hash": "349edb46ce5e7779537feb7f2a1bfd356b18c35cdbacffcd0f33eb2013a77156"}, {"id": "hf:2608.29753", "source": "huggingface", "kind": "paper", "url": "https://huggingface.co/papers/2608.29753", "title": "PAGE-RAG: Provenance-Aware Graph Evidence Promotion for Fixed-Budget Multi-hop Retrieval-Augmented Generation", "body": "Multi-hop question answering in retrieval-augmented gener?ation (RAG) often benefits from retrieving beyond the few candidates that will finally be read: narrow retrieval can miss an indispensable hop, while expanded retrieval introduces topical distractors. This challenge is not tied to a particu?lar knowledge-base format. Candidate pools may come from standalone retrievers, standard RAG backends, or graph-based retrieval pipelines. What is needed is a query-aware selection layer that can use relational structure to filter candidates be?fore generation. PAGE-RAG addresses this setting by using a graph as a temporary selection structure, rather than assum?ing a graph-structured knowledge base. It builds a query-local graph over retrieved candidates, records why candidates are connected, and treats each connection as a support hypothe?sis rather than support itself. We identify the resulting failure mode as a connectivity-support gap: connected candidates do not necessarily support the answer. We propose PAGE-RAG, a Provenance-Aware Graph Evidence promotion method that scores candidate paths with relevance, source-tracing meta?data, specificity, hubness, noise, and coherence signals, and applies minimal sufficient selection to promote supporting facts into a compact reader context. PAGE-RAG can serve as a complete retrieval-to-reading pipeline, and the same promo?tion stage can be inserted after existing retrieval or RAG sys?tems without replacing their upstream retrieval logic. Across three multi-hop QA benchmarks under the same final bud?get, PAGE-RAG improves support F1 and answer F1 by 10.4 and 3.3 points on a weighted average over a strong retriever. As a plug-in, PAGE-RAG further improves all reported RAG backends, including reasoning-oriented, compression-based, graph-based, and document/chunk-level systems.", "created_at": "2026-08-30T00:00:00.000Z", "updated_at": "2026-08-30T00:00:00.000Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:30:17Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": [], "metadata": {"read_depth": "abstract", "query": "candidate-only paged indexer scoring", "githubRepo": null, "arxiv_id": "2608.29753"}, "content_hash": "72efcde122767c12972eedb250b13abcf8813682cf2c3e9babbc072ba2f440d1"}, {"id": "hf:2603.26710", "source": "huggingface", "kind": "paper", "url": "https://huggingface.co/papers/2603.26710", "title": "Agentic AI for Human Resources: LLM-Driven Candidate Assessment", "body": "In this work, we present a modular and interpretable framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate candidate assessment in recruitment. The system integrates diverse sources, including job descriptions, CVs, interview transcripts, and HR feedback; to generate structured evaluation reports that mirror expert judgment. Unlike traditional ATS tools that rely on keyword matching or shallow scoring, our approach employs role-specific, LLM-generated rubrics and a multi-agent architecture to perform fine-grained, criteria-driven evaluations. The framework outputs detailed assessment reports, candidate comparisons, and ranked recommendations that are transparent, auditable, and suitable for real-world hiring workflows.\n  Beyond rubric-based analysis, we introduce an LLM-Driven Active Listwise Tournament mechanism for candidate ranking. Instead of noisy pairwise comparisons or inconsistent independent scoring, the LLM ranks small candidate subsets (mini-tournaments), and these listwise permutations are aggregated using a Plackett-Luce model. An active-learning loop selects the most informative subsets, producing globally coherent and sample-efficient rankings. This adaptation of listwise LLM preference modeling (previously explored in financial asset ranking) provides a principled and highly interpretable methodology for large-scale candidate ranking in talent acquisition.", "created_at": "2026-03-17T00:00:00.000Z", "updated_at": "2026-03-17T00:00:00.000Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:30:17Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": [], "metadata": {"read_depth": "abstract", "query": "candidate-only paged indexer scoring", "githubRepo": null, "arxiv_id": "2603.26710"}, "content_hash": "a7c8d384e968a5995f3835cd6e5976a194dc419339e9cf2d2d0884c24a572f25"}, {"id": "github:SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX:4272", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/pull/4272", "title": "perf(agentx): move Qwen3.8-Flash-Next H200 SGLang curve to v0.5.22 with TP2 points / \u6027\u80fd\uff1a\u5c06 Qwen3.8-Flash-Next H200 SGLang \u66f2\u7ebf\u8fc1\u79fb\u5230 v0.5.22 \u5e76\u65b0\u589e TP2 \u70b9", "body": "[by Claude Code]\n\nMoves `qwen3.8next-fp8-h200-sglang-agentic-mtp` from the model-branch image `lmsysorg/sglang:qwen38flashnext` to the `lmsysorg/sglang:v0.5.22-cu130` release, pinned by digest, and reshapes the H200 AgentX MTP curve:\n\n- **Decode CUDA graphs cover the full admission.** The recipe admitted 2x the concurrency but captured decode graphs only up to the concurrency, so any decode step with more running requests than the concurrency ran eager. "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/pull/477", "title": "[Fix][Einsum] Reject `c` in SM90 `fp8_einsum(\"bhr,hdr->bhd\")` to prevent silent data corruption in `sm90_fp8_bmm`", "body": "This PR fixes #476 .\n\n## Summary\n\nOn SM90, `deep_gemm.fp8_einsum(\"bhr,hdr->bhd\", a, b, d, c)` returns `a @ b.T` instead of `c + a @ b.T`: `c` is silently dropped.\n\n- The host first copies `c` into `d`, expecting the kernel to add `a @ b.T` onto `d` ([gemm.hpp L49\u2013L51](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/csrc/apis/gemm.hpp#L49-L51)).\n- On SM90, `sm90_fp8_bmm` launches `sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d_impl`, which cannot add onto `d`. It writes `d` with a plain TMA store ([sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.cuh L434](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/deep_gemm/include/deep_gemm/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.cuh#L434)), which overwrites `c`. `sm90_fp8_bmm` sets `with_accumulation` ([L300](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp#L300)), but never passes it to the kernel ([L36\u2013L61](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp#L36-L61)).\n- The other launcher of this kernel, `sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d`, rejects `c` ([L81](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp#L81)). `sm90_fp8_bmm` does not ([L290](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/blob/1fc9c66975ee322d628b3288534ca0e9ce12a64d/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp#L290)).\n\n`\"bhr,hdr->bhd\"` is the only `fp8_einsum` expression that runs on SM90, and `tests/test_einsum.py` passes `c` to it only on SM100, which accumulates correctly. \n\nReproduction: See Issue #476 .\n\n## Fix\n\nAdd the missing `not c.has_value()` check to `sm90_fp8_bmm`, as in `sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d`. Passing `c` on SM90 now raises an error instead of returning a wrong result. As with `sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d`, `d` has already been overwritten with `c` when the error is raised.\n\n```diff\n--- a/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp\n+++ b/csrc/jit_kernels/impls/sm90_fp8_gemm_1d2d.hpp\n@@ -287,7 +287,7 @@ static void sm90_fp8_bmm(const torch::Tensor& a, const torch::Tensor& sfa,\n                          const int& batch_size, const int& m, const int& n, const int& k,\n                          const cute::UMMA::Major& major_a, const cute::UMMA::Major& major_b, const cute::UMMA::Major& major_sfb,\n                          const std::string& compiled_dims) {\n-    DG_HOST_ASSERT(d.scalar_type() == torch::kBFloat16);\n+    DG_HOST_ASSERT(not c.has_value() and d.scalar_type() == torch::kBFloat16);\n     DG_HOST_ASSERT(major_a == cute::UMMA::Major::K and major_b == cute::UMMA::Major::K);\n \n     const auto desc = GemmDesc {\n```\n\n## Tests\n\n`test_fp8_bhr_hdr_bhd` already checks `c` on SM100. This PR adds the SM90 counterpart: a call with `c` must raise. The cluster path is enabled from a vocabulary size of 128K, based on measurements on an 8\u00d7H20 machine. SM100 and SM103 continue to use the existing 16-CTA cluster and 512K threshold.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- Add `DEEP_SELECT_CUDA_ARCHS` to select `90a`, `100a`, and/or `103a` at build time.\r\n- Keep the default build targets unchanged: `sm_100a` and `sm_103a`.\r\n- Add eight SM90 cluster instantiations covering:\r\n  - `int32` and `int64` output indices\r\n  - `sorted_index` enabled and disabled\r\n  - `return_value` enabled and disabled\r\n- Dispatch SM90 BF16 workloads to an 8-CTA cluster when:\r\n  - `batch_size <= 6`\r\n  - `vocab_size >= 128K`\r\n  - `topk <= 1024`\r\n- Preserve the existing 16-CTA cluster and 512K threshold for SM100/SM103.\r\n- Fall back to `nvidia-smi -L` for CUDA platform detection when `lspci` is unavailable in a container.\r\n- Avoid the `std::format` dependency in the C++ binding so the extension builds with older host standard libraries commonly found in CUDA containers.\r\n- Document the SM90 build command:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nDEEP_SELECT_CUDA_ARCHS=90a pip install -v .\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Performance\r\n\r\nMeasured on NVIDIA H20 with BF16 input, `batch_size` 1 and 6, and `topk` 512 and 1024. Each result is the median of 100 cold-cache runs.\r\n\r\nThe improvement column compares the new 8-CTA cluster with the existing single-CTA path compiled for SM90.\r\n\r\n| Vocabulary size | Improvement over single CTA | Speedup over `torch.topk` |\r\n|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| 128K | 25.2%\u201335.0% | 3.11\u00d7\u20133.83\u00d7 |\r\n| 256K | 36.5%\u201344.6% | 2.79\u00d7\u20133.57\u00d7 |\r\n| 512K | 52.4%\u201356.1% | 2.66\u00d7\u20133.75\u00d7 |\r\n| 1M | 61.5%\u201362.3% | 2.12\u00d7\u20134.11\u00d7 |\r\n\r\nShort-sequence and large-batch workloads continue to use the existing normal kernel. Their measured speedup over `torch.topk` remains 4.88\u00d7\u20136.27\u00d7.\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\n\r\n- Clean SM90 build with CUDA 13.0 and PyTorch 2.11.\r\n- `cuobjdump` register-spill check A replica-deterministic sample sets a value\n  threshold and a 256-bin scale; every CTA walks its slice once; the last-arriving CTA merges\n  the bin-major publish tables and resolves the crossing bin with an exact key-space select.\n  Rows up to 16K columns never enter that pipeline: a census arm (<= 4K) and a register-resident\n  arm (4K to 16K) solve them in one CTA. Floods past the tie capacity are refined from the\n  crossing bin's exact key range; anything else falls through to a fused exact re-solve on\n  device, so every call stays sync-free and CUDA-graph capturable.\n- `radix_primitives` \u2014 SGLang's coarse-histogram structure made exact (no tie truncation),\n  fp32/fp16/bf16, grouped rows (`next_n`) and compressed columns (`compress_ratio`), optional\n  values, multi-CTA long rows.\n- `cutlass_primitives` \u2014 the vendored cutlass-primitives library (`VENDORED.md`, Apache-2.0):\n  register, clustered-register and streaming kernels with a census split for large k; the\n  full-row leader. `flashinfer.topk_varlen` exports its workspace helpers\n  (`cutlass_primitives_workspace_bytes`, `cutlass_primitives_row_order`) and\n  `release_cutlass_primitives_resources`.\n- `sglang` \u2014 SGLang's own DeepSeek-V4 indexer top-k (Apache-2.0, vendored verbatim from upstream\n  commit 7f27bf4708; `csrc/sglang_dsv4/VENDORED.md`, `licenses/LICENSE.sglang.txt`) under the\n  same contract, including a paged variant: the same kernel SGLang runs, for parity and for the\n  check that the FlashInfer API adds no overhead over the raw kernel. It is exact only while the\n  rank-k bin holds at most 2048 candidates, so `auto` never selects it.\n\n**Fused paged output.** `top_k_varlen(..., page_table=table, page_size=64)` returns physical\nKV slots `page_table[q, c // page_size] * page_size + c % page_size` from the selection launch\nitself (the SGLang DSA decode contract, one kernel instead of a top-k plus a transform pass).\nThe table is a contiguous, 16-byte-aligned int32 `(requests, max_pages)` tensor covering every\nrequest's valid length; it may be narrower than the logits width, and rows past its coverage\nare clamped to the covered prefix. Served by `walkfirst_primitives`, `radix_primitives` and\n`sglang`; `backend=\"auto\"` with a page table ranks the two exact primitives backends (they are\noutside the unpaged ranking).\n\n**Also in this PR:** a fix to the pre-existing multi-CTA `radix` kernel (chunks shorter than\ntheir misaligned prefix read past the valid length and wrote shared memory at negative offsets;\nCTA 0 could reset the group's arrival counter while a time-sliced peer still spun on it; the\nper-group state grows by one int, STATE_SIZE 770 to 771, allocated internally with a bumped\ncache key), the trace template for `top_k_varlen` extended with the paged inputs (the page size\nnames the paged definitions), the benchmarks that produced the numbers below, the four new\nbackends in `benchmarks/routines/topk_varlen.py`, and a one-line fix to\n`tests/trace/example.py` (an upstream call passed a `str` device to `is_sm100a_supported`, which\ncrashes the example on sm_100 hosts).\n\n**Why.** The DSA indexer runs one top-k per layer per decode step; SGLang today uses its own\nfused kernel. These backends are exact where that kernel approximates, faster on every decode\ncell we measured across six architectures, and fuse the page-table output so an engine needs\none launch. The package has the per-step kernels (pre-indexer, paged scorer, route expansion and mapping, output gate) and three objects that run them out of caller-owned workspaces, so a captured CUDA graph can replay a whole step: `QSASelection`, `QSAAttention` and `QSA`. `qsa_capabilities()` tells a serving stack what the build carries.\n\nTwo changes to existing code support it:\n\n- `BlockSparseAttentionWrapper` runs on a paged KV cache (`plan(..., kv_cache_page_size=...)`; dense, FP8 or NVFP4; NHD or HND) and reports its workspace size before planning, so several live plans can share one arena. It also fixes the default FP8 scales, which were sized by the block width instead of the KV-head count.\n- `top_k_ragged_transform` takes `out`, `workspace` and a resolved `backend`, so a caller can run it without allocating and with the backend fixed up front. Calls without them are unchanged.\n\nThe kernels run on SM80 and every later architecture: the JIT and AOT builds target 8.x through 12.x, and there are no architecture-specific paths, so newer GPUs run the same mma.sync and cp.async code rather than tuned variants. SM7x is excluded because the scorer's m16n8k16 arrived with SM80. The pre-indexer, scorer and route are one JIT module; the gate is a second one built without fast math, so a subnormal logistic is not flushed to zero. `cp_async.cuh` gains one L1-caching copy and `mma.cuh` the m16n8k16 helpers; existing functions are untouched.\n\n#5859, #5860 and #5861 were folded into this PR and closed.\n\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\n\nTracked in #5669.\n\n## \ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\n\nThank you for contributing to FlashInfer! Sparse decode uses bf16 q and fp8 e4m3 KV pages and covers every admitted coordinate. Sparse prefill uses bf16 q, supports GQA groups 4 / 8 / 16 and handles up to 4096 pages per sequence. Proxy score has two regimes. The decode regime takes fp8 and bf16 q, a one-head index cache, Hq in {1,2,4} and max_seqlen_q <= 4. The prefill regime takes fp8 q with both accumulation precisions. Top-k block select has one form per planner coordinate: columns per CTA, tile groups, index bits and mask form. One route table serves 89 generated programs: 22 sparse-decode, 6 sparse-prefill (3 prefill_v1 + 3 prefill_v2), 24 proxy-decode, 5 proxy-prefill and 32 top-k.\n\nThis PR delivers the Hopper (sm_90a) variant of the Cake MSA family. That covers all four operations and all 89 programs, compiled with FlashInfer's sm_90a flags. No earlier Cake version of this family ships on sm_90a. So in every row the baseline (`fi_sm90`) is FlashInfer's existing SM90 CuTe DSL kernel for the same operation and shape, measured in the same session on the same GPU. Speedup = fi_sm90 latency / Cake latency, so a value > 1.000 means Cake is faster. All rows were measured on an NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3 (compute capability 9.0, sm_90a, 132 SMs) with CUDA 13.3 and torch 2.13.0a0+9186a08b2c.nv26.07.\n\n*NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3 (compute capability 9.0, sm_90a, 132 SMs), CUDA 13.3, torch 2.13.0a0+9186a08b2c.nv26.07; same-session interleaved CUPTI medians (cold L2, 3 reps) of the Cake kernel and the fi_sm90 CuTe DSL kernel of the same shape; speedup = fi_sm90 / Cake (> 1.000 = Cake faster); correctness-only shapes carry no timing.*\n\n### Top-k block select (MSA indexer) (28 shapes)\n\n| shape | Cake (us) | fi_sm90 (us) | speedup fi_sm90 / Cake "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/53563", "title": "[RFC]: SM90 Exact Fused DSA Prefill Indexer", "body": "### Motivation.\n\nWe propose an optional SM90 backend for vLLM's `SparseAttnIndexer` prefill path. It computes FP8 scores and exact TopK in one CUDA kernel without materializing the full score tensor or intermediate candidates in HBM.\n\nThe first implementation PR targets only the H64, D=128, topk=2048, single-GPU FP8 prefill path used by DeepSeek V3.2. The existing path remains the default. H32 support will follow in a separate PR.\n\nThe current vLLM prefill indexer follows this high-level flow:\n\n```text\ngather K -> fp8_fp4_mqa_logits -> materialize logits -> top_k_per_row_prefill\n```\n\nAt long context lengths, writing and reading the full logits tensor and the extra kernel boundary become significant. In our current H20 matrix, the `H=1M, B=32, Q=512` case has a 508.25 MiB peak score chunk and materializes 64.03 GiB of scores over one indexer call, while the final TopK index output is 128 MiB.\n\nThe goal is to replace only the score + TopK stage and eliminate full-score HBM materialization without changing downstream Sparse MLA attention.\n\n### Proposed Change.\n\n#### Scope and integration\n\nThe new backend plugs into the existing `sparse_attn_indexer()` prefill branch:\n\n```text\nexisting Q quantization, KV-cache insert, and chunk metadata\n                              |\n                              v\n                 cp_gather_indexer_k_quant_cache\n                              |\n                              v\n                 SM90 fused score + exact TopK\n                              |\n                              v\n                   existing topk_indices_buffer\n```\n\nThe first PR is intentionally limited to:\n\n- SM90a, validated separately on H20, H800, and H100;\n- the DeepSeek V3.2 FP8 prefill path;\n- H64, D=128, and topk=2048;\n- single-GPU execution without DCP or PCP;\n- INT32 output shaped `[M, 2048]`, with unspecified order.\n\nDecode, KV-cache layout, K gather, and Sparse MLA attention remain unchanged.\n\nThe proposed configuration is:\n\n```python\nattention_config.indexer_prefill_backend = \"default\" | \"sm90_exact\"\n```\n\n`default` preserves the existing vLLM implementation. `sm90_exact` explicitly enables the experimental backend. Selecting `sm90_exact` reports a clear initialization error when GPU, dtype, H/D/topk, DCP/PCP, or shared-memory requirements are not met.\n\nRows shorter than topk use the existing short-sequence path and `-1` sentinel rule. A row exactly equal to topk returns all valid indices without reading Q/K.\n\nDCP is excluded because the current DCP merge consumes both local TopK scores and indices, while this kernel outputs indices only.\n\n#### Kernel design\n\nThe current H64 kernel uses a one-query-per-CTA pipeline:\n\n```text\nTMA K/K-scale double buffer\n        -> 4 WGMMA math warp groups (256 keys/round)\n        -> generation-tagged shared-memory score ring\n        -> FP32 ordered-bit histogram/radix cutoff\n        -> running Top-2048 and final indices\n```\n\nThe main implementation parameters are:\n\n- 768 threads per CTA;\n- four WGMMA math warp groups;\n- two TMA stages;\n- 12,544 scores per selection group and two ring slots;\n- 704 threads participating in the final drain;\n- 80 registers per thread;\n- 232,448 bytes of dynamic shared memory and 1,024 bytes of static shared memory;\n- zero stack and local-memory usage in the production cubin.\n\nShared-memory usage gives one CTA per SM on H20. H20, H800, and H100 share one SM90a source, while eligibility and performance are validated separately on each device instead of maintaining SKU-specific implementations.\n\nThe prototype uses runtime NVCC for iteration speed. The upstream version will retain only the selected production variant and use vLLM's static CUDA build, with no first-request JIT compilation.\n\n#### Exactness contract\n\n`exact` means that the selector computes an exact cutoff for the FP32 scores produced by the fused kernel. For a valid interval, let `c` be its 2048th-largest score. A valid output must:\n\n- contain 2048 unique indices from the valid interval;\n- include every index with `score > c`;\n- include no index with `score < c`;\n- fill the remaining positions from indices with `score == c`.\n\nOutput order and the specific choice among cutoff ties are unstable and are not part of the API contract. This matches unordered TopK set semantics.\n\nThe score expression is arithmetically equivalent to the current FP8 indexer, but WGMMA and DeepGEMM may use different floating-point reduction orders. Validation must therefore separate selector exactness relative to the fused scores from numerical error and model outputs relative to the latest vLLM backend. This RFC does not claim bitwise equivalence between those score producers.\n\n#### Related work\n\n- **Current vLLM:** materializes full logits in HBM before TopK and serves as the current baseline.\n- **[#52696](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52696):** materializes FP16 logits and uses native FP16 TopK, reducing the size of the logits tensor without removing its HBM round trip.\n- **[#48726](", "created_at": null, "updated_at": "2026-08-25T02:51:03Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T21:46:55Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "tags": ["h100", "deepseek", "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62463", "title": "[Perf][SM90] Score only candidate blocks in the DSv4.1 decode indexer", "body": "\n\n\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd a Triton candidate-only decode scoring path for DeepSeek V4.1 on SM90,\nreading FP8 K directly from the paged indexer cache instead of scoring the\nfull context and masking it afterwards.\n\nRelated to #61830. This is a separate implementation from the gather-based\nprototype in draft #62000: it avoids the intermediate candidate-K buffer.\nThe flag is off by default (`VLLM_DSA_CANDIDATE_PAGED_DECODE=1` to enable).\n\n## Claims\n\n- On H20 TP8 with ~1M input tokens, the evalscope A/B below measured a\n  **9.6\u201323.8% reduction in mean ITL** across concurrency 1/2/4/8.\n- Consumer logits use 16,384 candidate columns instead of the full compressed\n  context. The candidate-source layers, pre-candidate layers and prefill path\n  are unchanged.\n- Path selection uses configuration checks, not capture-time context lengths.\n  Live lengths and page tables remain device inputs during CUDA graph replay.\n\nThese results cover the measured long-context workload, not a general\nno-regression claim. Accuracy and shorter-workload checks are still pending.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Environment\n\n- Machine: 8x NVIDIA H20 96GB, TP8; driver 535.161.08, CUDA 13.0\n- Software: torch 2.14.0+cu130, triton 3.8.0, deep_gemm 2.8.1, vLLM at this\n  PR's commit\n- Server: `--tensor-parallel-size 8 --max-model-len 1048576 --max-num-seqs 16\n  --enable-prefix-caching`, speculative decoding with\n  `num_speculative_tokens=5`. The A/B runs differ only in\n  `VLLM_DSA_CANDIDATE_PAGED_DECODE` (0 vs 1)\n\n### Test guards\n\n```bash\npytest -q tests/kernels/attention/test_candidate_paged_logits.py\n```\n\nThe tests cover:\n\n- **Scores and selection:** candidate logits against an FP32 reference\n  (`rtol=2e-2`, `atol=1e-2`), plus selected-set comparisons for `persistent`\n  and `auto` top-k. This is kernel-level coverage, not model accuracy.\n- **Cache layout:** page gaps of 0/64/260 bytes with block sizes 16/64;\n  invalid cache entry widths are rejected.\n- **Bounds:** empty contexts, padded/out-of-range candidate blocks, and\n  per-row causal limits during speculative decoding.\n- **CUDA graphs:** capture on a short context, replay with changed lengths\n  and page tables, then compare against eager execution.\n- **Gating and workspace:** default-off behavior, rejected configurations,\n  and reuse of the reserved workspace without growth during capture.\n- **Performance smoke test:** an effective-bandwidth threshold over the\n  candidate path, intended to catch severe regressions, not to measure\n  isolated HBM bandwidth.\n\nThe current L4 attention CI job skips the SM90 GPU cases. A saved H20 pytest\nreport for the submitted revision is still needed; the coverage above does\nnot imply those cases passed in CI.\n\n### End-to-end A/B (1M context)\n\nI ran both configurations on the same commit with the same client arguments.\nThere is one run per configuration, with 2/4/8/16 measured requests at\nconcurrency 1/2/4/8 respectively. Q remains resident across assigned chunks from the same group.\nAdd a three-stage pipeline:\ncp.async\nloads packed tile i+2 while CUDA cores decode tile i+1 and WGMMA consumes tile i. Measurements below show that the best mode depends on shape and visibility.\nScheduler metadata is CTA-local shared memory, recomputed on every invocation and graph replay. There is no CPU length readback, global allocation/counter, or request-slot scratch. "github", "kind": "resource", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/humming/issues/38", "title": "[Performance] MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 with group-32 activation scales is slow on H100 \u00b7 Issue #38 \u00b7 vllm-project/humming \u00b7 GitHub", "body": "[Performance] MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 with group-32 activation scales is slow on H100\n#38\nNew issue\nCopy link\nNew issue\nCopy link\nClosed as not planned\nClosed as not planned\n[Performance] MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 with group-32 activation scales is slow on H100\n#38\nCopy link\nDescription\nhit-zhengbao\nopened\non Jul 13, 2026\nIssue body actions\nWe are evaluating an MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 GEMM on NVIDIA H100.\nThe fused-E8M0 Humming kernel is numerically correct, but with group-32\nMXFP8 activation scales it is significantly slower than both:\nHumming's generic group-scale path\nA QDQ baseline that dequantizes both operands and then runs a BF16 GEMM\nWe would like to ask whether group-32 MXFP8 input scaling is an intended\nperformance target and whether there are plans to optimize this case on Hopper.\nQuantization format\nThe operands use the following format:\nActivation payload: FP8 E4M3\nActivation scale: per-token, per-K-group power-of-two scale\nActivation group size: 32\nWeight payload: MXFP4 E2M1\nWeight scale: E8M0\nWeight group size: 32\nOutput dtype: BF16\nThis is an MXFP8-style activation representation: each 32-element K group\ncontains an E4M3 payload and one power-of-two scale.\nHumming receives the decoded real FP32 activation scales through\ninput_scale\n. The original activation scales are exactly representable\nas E8M0 powers of two.\nThis differs from the DeepEP grouped-FP8 case added by\n#37\n:\nPR\nfix: support grouped FP8 MXFP4\n#37\nactivation group size: 128\nThis case activation group size: 32\nBoth cases use MXFP4 weights with E8M0 group size 32\nEnvironment\nGPU: NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3\nHumming commit tested:\n79c1cef\n/\n#35\nOutput dtype: BF16\nWarmup: 20 iterations\nMeasurement: 200 iterations\nTiming: CUDA events and CUDA Graph replay\nGEMM operation count:\n2 * M * N * K\nPR\n#37\nwas merged after the tested commit. Based on its diff, it adds the\ngroup-128 integration and fixes non-fused scale tail decoding, but does\nnot appear to change fused WGMMA accumulator scheduling or the H100\nheuristics. We can confirm the measurements on the latest main if needed.\nHumming configuration\nThe fused kernel is configured approximately as follows:\nmeta\n=\nHummingLayerMeta\n(\nshape_n\n=\nN\n,\nshape_k\n=\nK\n,\na_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e4m3\n,\nb_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat4e2m1\n,\nc_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nbfloat16\n,\nbs_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e8m0\n,\ninput_scale_group_size\n=\n32\n,\nweight_scale_group_size\n=\n32\n,\nweight_scale_type\n=\nWeightScaleType\n.\nGROUP_TENSOR\n,\nuse_fused_e8m0_scale\n=\nTrue\n,\nmma_type\n=\n\"wgmma\"\n,\n)\nThe weight and E8M0 scale are prepared through the same fused preprocessing\nused by\nHummingLayerMethod.transform_humming_layer\n.\nThe input scale passed to\nhumming_gemm\nis FP32 with shape\n[M, K / 32]\n.\nCorrectness\nThe following implementations were compared against an FP32 reference:\nFused-E8M0 Humming\nGeneric Humming group-scale path\nQDQ followed by BF16 GEMM\nAll tested correctness cases passed.\nQDQ baseline\nThe QDQ latency includes activation dequantization, weight dequantization,\nand the BF16 GEMM. It is not a GEMM-only baseline.\nEquivalent pseudocode:\na_scale_fp32\n=\ntorch\n.\nexp2\n(\na_scale_e8m0_u8\n.\nfloat\n()\n-\n127\n)\nw_scale_fp32\n=\ntorch\n.\nexp2\n(\nw_scale_e8m0_u8\n.\nfloat\n()\n-\n127\n)\na_dequant\n=\n(\na_fp8\n.\nfloat\n()\n*\na_scale_fp32\n.\nrepeat_interleave\n(\n32\n,\ndim\n=\n1\n)\n).\nto\n(\ntorch\n.\nbfloat16\n)\nw_dequant\n=\n(\ndecode_mxfp4_e2m1\n(\nw_mxfp4_packed\n)\n*\nw_scale_fp32\n.\nrepeat_interleave\n(\n32\n,\ndim\n=\n1\n)\n*\nglobal_scale\n).\nto\n(\ntorch\n.\nbfloat16\n)\noutput\n=\ntorch\n.\nmatmul\n(\na_dequant\n,\nw_dequant\n.\nT\n)\nAll operations shown above are inside the timed region.\nPerformance results\nCUDA Graph replay results:\nShape\n(M,N,K)\nQDQ core\nGeneric Humming\nFused-E8M0 Humming\nFused vs generic\nFused vs QDQ\n(512,3072,4096)\n0.299 ms\n0.048 ms\n0.637 ms\n13.3x slower\n2.1x slower\n(4096,3072,4096)\n0.580 ms\n0.965 ms\n4.969 ms\n5.1x slower\n8.6x slower\n(8192,3072,4096)\n0.893 ms\n1.911 ms\n9.681 ms\n5.1x slower\n10.8x slower\n(512,14336,4096)\n1.256 ms\n0.191 ms\n2.864 ms\n15.0x slower\n2.3x slower\n(4096,14336,4096)\n1.900 ms\n4.448 ms\n22.298 ms\n5.0x slower\n11.7x slower\n(4096,4096,7168)\n1.201 ms\n2.221 ms\n11.230 ms\n5.1x slower\n9.4x slower\nFor large-M shapes, the fused kernel reaches only approximately\n20-22 effective TFLOPS.\nSuspected bottleneck\nOur current understanding is:\nFor FP8,\nkPartMmaShapeK\nis 32.\nWith\ninput_scale_group_size=32\n, every WGMMA step reaches an activation\nscale boundary.\nscale_d\nis therefore false at every K=32 step.\nThe current path performs\nwgmma_commit()\nfollowed by\nwgmma_wait<0>()\n, register fencing, and accumulator scaling for every\nK=32 group.\nThe fused H100 heuristic selects a relatively large\n128x128x128\nbase tile, which may increase the cost of processing the accumulator\nat each group boundary.\nWith the group-128 input scales used by PR\n#37\n, four K=32 WGMMA operations\ncan accumulate before applying the activation scale. The synchronization\nand accumulator-processing overhead can therefore be better amortized.\nThis is only our interpretation of the current implementation; please\ncorrect us if we are m", "created_at": null, "updated_at": null, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:23:32Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:vllm-project/humming:38", "tags": ["h100", "deepseek-v4.1-flash", "deepseek", "throughput", "performance", "communication"], "metadata": {"id": "github:vllm-project/humming:38", "repo": "vllm-project/humming", "number": 38, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:23:31Z", "discussion_coverage": "HTML-visible only; not exhaustive formal reviews or paginated replies", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/humming/issues/38", "html_sha256": "708d97e55b44e67c91643512e29adfadeab119f819a127a6a57e3044b07825a5", "text_sha256": "ca6f7d0c248f98d96930e03956800d67929fb4a78f90c6daac8141bba121668b", "characters": 11736, "title": "[Performance] MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 with group-32 activation scales is slow on H100 \u00b7 Issue #38 \u00b7 vllm-project/humming \u00b7 GitHub"}, "content_hash": "4259395570a541b2a26eb5cae040e321f96019d79a715a1e842313f4f89b0551", "rank": -5.914505286909181}], "memory": [{"id": "hypothesis:9ea2ed3a2aa40010bff0a8ebec9108067bba603b0fdcf542efd1be9ff9fcf8fc", "status": "deferred", "fingerprint": "789eb31eaf7bf44f4db3926d8724b35c624b68c3ff8fb23b6255a9854dcdb22b", "statement": "For the two TP1/DP4/EP4 engines, first inspect the effective image, router policy, API2 request path, and internal DP rank selection to establish whether rank-aware prefix routing is already active.
